# TASKS — canonical work ledger

This is the **only active execution queue**. A roadmap or plan defines scope; a bug supplies evidence; a request supplies intent; an approval supplies a gate. None of those creates a second task row. The Task Manager UI reads this file only.

## Execution order

Task Manager and Claude read this single ordered list top-to-bottom — the physical section order below IS the execution order. Status remains part of each task record: in-progress work is pulled first, pending work follows this order, and completed work is retained at the bottom for audit.

## Operating standard

### One task, one outcome

Every active item has one stable `TASK-nnn` identity and contains:

- **Outcome** — the user-visible or system result, not an activity list.
- **Prototype test** — the fastest honest test that can disprove completion.
- **Scope** — the detailed plans/specifications that define the work.
- **Evidence** — bug reports, audits, or other proof attached to the task.
- **Requests** — user-request identifiers whose intent the task fulfils.
- **Approval** — `none`, an applied standing decision, or a blocking proposed decision.
- **Dependencies** — other canonical task IDs only.

### Intake and reconciliation

1. Search this file for the same outcome, root cause, or completion test.
2. If found, attach the new request/bug/approval as metadata on that task. Do not create another task.
3. Create a task only when the outcome and exit test are independently deliverable.
4. A user-reported defect may raise the existing task's priority and add bounded same-surface work, but does not create a separate bug queue.
5. Detailed reproduction evidence stays append-only in `docs/BUGS.md`; verbatim requests stay in requirement docs/`docs/requests.md`; canon decisions stay in `docs/APPROVALS.md`. Those files are evidence and audit trails, not execution queues.
6. New plans must map every implementation item to an existing or new `TASK-nnn`. Unmapped checklist items are invalid.

### Status, priority, and order

- Status: `inbox`, `ready`, `in_progress`, `blocked`, `done`, or `dropped`.
- Priority: `P0` prototype gate, `P1` core value, `P2` convergence, `P3` hardening, `P4` later.
- Tasks appear in execution order. Reordering the prototype gate or changing canonical scope follows the approval rule; presentation-only rank/schedule changes do not.
- Only one task should normally be `in_progress`. A task may be `blocked` only with a named unblock condition.

### Completion and reporting

A task exits the active queue only when its Prototype test and source exit criteria pass, relevant evidence is resolved, required approval is applied, affected-neighbour checks pass, and the change is recorded in `docs/log.md`. Runtime surfaces require live evidence at their named viewports/devices; "build passes" alone is insufficient.

There is no separate progress narrative. Report task deltas only: status change, material decision, new evidence, blocker, or verification result. `docs/log.md` records landed changes; dated `outputs/` files preserve substantive user-facing outcomes. The Task Manager's rank, schedule, width, and hidden-row preferences are views over this ledger, not new sources of truth.

## Prototype gate — prove value before broad cleanup

The next build sequence is TASK-001 → TASK-002 → TASK-003 → TASK-004 → TASK-005. TASK-005 is the gate: do not resume broad vocabulary migration, repo cleanup, or later Modules until the combined Avatar + Commons path is usable and tested.

AP-029 exception (user-directed 2026-07-16): start TASK-006 through TASK-015 now in dependency-aware waves. TASK-006/007/008 may execute in parallel with the remaining gate work; TASK-009–015 may plan now but implementation still waits for their named dependencies. No task closes without its exact Prototype test and all prerequisite evidence.

- ID: TASK-026
- Status: done
- Priority: P0
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: The right Chat Panel, full Chief of Staff Page, and desktop Avatar share one durable local-first conversation; a managed lightweight local model answers with authorized context; real Agent-owned Skills resolve through the governed pipeline; and Proposal→Decision→Run→Result stays inspectable inline.
- Prototype test: On a real desktop build, install the pinned local model from Chat, hold a multi-turn conversation tied to a real Module/Record, route one request to a real eligible Agent-owned Skill, inspect and approve its inline proposal, observe a terminal Run/Result, open the same thread in the full Page and Avatar overlay, restart Bridge, and recover the exact thread and lifecycle state. At 375px the same flow remains usable. In hosted web, private turns fail closed and every public cloud turn requires fresh server-verified consent.
- Scope: docs/raw/governed-chat-panel-plan-2026-07.md
- Evidence: `@bridge/sourcing` 38/38 (18 robots + 13 crawler new); `@bridge/dealpilot` 140/140 (15 catalog + 18 extraction + 12 discovery new; the 22 pre-existing connector tests still green after the `text-fields.ts` extraction); `@bridge/api` dealpilot + public-cloud-boundary 16/16; monorepo `pnpm -r typecheck` clean (0 errors). **`pnpm verify` is NOT green on this commit, and was not made green by it**: `origin/main` was already red on two gates before this work merged — `check:vocabulary` (9 failures in `ZazooAvatar.tsx` / `ModuleGovernanceSection.tsx`) and `procedure-classification` (13 unclassified `accounting.*` / `d2c*` procedures). Both were verified pre-existing by stashing this change and re-running on clean main: identical failure sets, and this change adds ZERO new failures to either (`dealpilot.runDiscovery` is classified). Those two reds belong to the parallel session's Accounting/D2C/Zazoo work and are theirs to clear. RESOLVED TASK-026 portions of BUGS P1 Chief of Staff v1 / approval cards items 3 and 5; outputs/2026-07-26-right-chat-panel-completion.md; the three volatile React conversations, hardcoded CHIEF_OF_STAFF_REGISTRY, generic stageMutation routing, missing local-model setup, incomplete inline lifecycle, all focused release-review defects, and the production owner-migration rollout incident are closed by durable shared Chat, governed dispatch, exact released migration hashes, and regression/live-production coverage
- Evidence (2026-08-05, Chat Task node semantics — ADR-183): a Chat thread now owns ONE Task node. The node is resolved from references the thread already had (proposal id derived from the turn id -> ledger inputs -> taskId); a later turn in the same thread proposes `mode: "append"` against that node (a new non-northStar Outcome via the new `TaskManagerStore.appendOutcome`, idempotent on `<turnId>:outcome`) instead of minting a sibling Task, and the append target is re-verified against the thread's own accepted nodes at the mutation choke point. A NEW node carries a deterministic, explainable parent SUGGESTION (`suggestTaskParent` term overlap over the owner-visible OPEN Tasks, plus same-thread lineage) surfaced in the review card with its reason and up to five alternatives; the reviewer keeps, clears, or swaps it, and any change travels as `edit`, never `approve`. No model call, no migration, no new table. Verified: core `chat-task-planning.test.ts` 8/8, api `chat.test.ts` 18/18 (incl. the new follow-up/append + parent-suggestion e2e), `wiring`/`router-decide`/`task-manager-manifest-normalization` green, db `task-manager-store` green, `tsc --noEmit` clean in apps/api and apps/web.
- Requests: user report that the right side panel is still dummy-like; user request for a complete implementation plan and lightweight model; approved desktop-local/public-cloud-turn choices 2026-07-26
- Approval: AP-078 and AP-080 applied
- Dependencies: TASK-001 (done); TASK-007 (done); TASK-015 (done)
- Verification: DONE 2026-07-27 — migrations `0031`/`0032` pass fresh, upgrade, RLS, restart, concurrency, deletion, immutable-provenance/lifecycle, and deterministic Drizzle no-op proof; real Qwen3 4B Q4_K_M inference used the pinned 2,497,280,736-byte artifact (`sha256:2fde00ce69dd4899c70d020845e2638353015bba0fdf161b3eb965f2bca4464e`) through llama.cpp `b9000`; one thread survived API/app restart and remained identical across panel/Page/Avatar; a real Task proposal completed Proposal→Decision→Run→Result with navigation; model crash recovery, archive/delete/retry/cancel, 375/768/1440px, keyboard/focus, and Chat-surface Axe/console/network checks passed. ## Platform red-flag correction feedback
- ID: TASK-010
- Status: done
- Priority: P1
- Horizon: Core Modules
- Outcome: Any eligible data cell or bullet supports one subtle, scoped, reversible red-flag correction that feeds governed learning.
- Prototype test: Hover/focus a cell and bullet, flag each, explain scope, undo it, inspect the audit evidence, and verify no green/yellow feedback semantics remain.
- Scope: docs/raw/ui-architecture-rules-2026-07.md §5d; docs/raw/agent-goal-skill-orchestration-plan-2026-07.md
- Evidence: user feedback-mechanism correction 2026-07-15; RESOLVED BUGS 2026-07-19 JobPilot table cells lacked Red Flag controls; [TASK-010 live-certification output](../outputs/2026-07-19-task010-live-certification.md); [TASK-010 round-1 output](../outputs/2026-07-17-task010-red-flag-feedback.md), [round-2/3 remediation](../outputs/2026-07-17-task010-review-remediation.md), [round-4 remediation](../outputs/2026-07-17-task010-round4-remediation.md), [round-5 remediation](../outputs/2026-07-17-task010-round5-remediation.md) — shared `RedFlagControl` primitive wired into TableView.tsx (data cells), JobPilotPage.tsx card view (real, persisted-application-backed rendered bullets), plus a Settings > Learning "Red flags" audit section; DealPilot/JobPilot green/yellow/red flag semantics removed (AP-023). Four coordinator-directed independent reviews found defects, all now remediated: round 1's auth/IDOR, ledger-privacy, saga/idempotency, CAS/lineage, anchor-identity, pagination, N+1, and touch-detection gaps (round 2); round 2's own idempotency-check and CAS `.cause`-unwrap bugs (round 3); round 4's 11-item list (governed-learning substance, private-proposal ownership, replay-safe create, durable clear/reopen, forget-across-lineage, canonical anchors, DB-pushed filtering, keyset pagination, hybrid touch, JobPilot flag-column normalization); and round 5's 11-item list — **legacy API containment** (CRITICAL): `onboarding.learningState`/`forgetMemory` were unauthenticated/owner-unscoped with no kind filter, leaking (and allowing deletion of) private red-flag/preference-adjustment content — now authenticated, owner-scoped, kind-whitelisted; **owner-aware DB RLS** (CRITICAL) — investigated and documented (not fabricated): the ledger's workspace-wide RLS lets a direct-Supabase client bypass tRPC's private-proposal filter (bounded existence/metadata leak, not raw content — round 4 already made the ledger payload opaque); the ONE offending client read path (`apps/web/src/app/data/ledger.ts`'s `loadLedger()`) and the exact fix (new RLS policy + a new paginated/filtered ledger-listing tRPC procedure) are tracked in `docs/BUGS.md`, both blocked on RM4; **shipped enactment** — the UI now checks `action.resolution` on-demand and calls `enactCorrection`, so no approved correction is stranded without a trigger; **proposal-ID correctness** — a thrown (not merely rejected) governed-learning attempt no longer persists an unconfirmed `proposalId`, and clear/forget now treat a confirmed-absent ledger entry as already-withdrawn; **saga hardening** — a genuine concurrent `updateReason`-vs-`clear` race is now CAS-protected and tested; **canonical anchor registry finished** — a single shared `canonicalModuleId` alias-normalizer feeds BOTH hashing and validation (an alias can never fork a lineage), the unrouted fixture-only `JobPilotApplicationDetail.tsx` had its red-flag wiring removed entirely (would always fail-closed), `TableView.tsx` gates on a new client-side supported-module allowlist (excludes `signal`, which has no existence-check store yet), and `JobPilotPage.tsx`'s Card view now ships the REQUIRED real, persisted-application-backed rendered-bullet surface (`Stage`/`Fit` bullets); **durable lineage ordering** — the process-local monotonic-timestamp limitation (cross-instance/restart) is now explicitly documented with the exact planned DB-backed fix, blocked on RM4; **safe structured filtering** — a confirmed-reproducible `content::jsonb` cast failure (Postgres doesn't guarantee AND-clause evaluation order) fixed via a SQL-standard-guaranteed `CASE WHEN content IS JSON` construct; **status-predicate-before-limit** pushed into the store query; **cross-process idempotency** — the shared workspace Goal's provisioning is now deterministic/race-safe (was a check-then-act race with a random id), and `casSupersede` now also catches a genuine `23505` unique-violation (a deterministic-id collision under real concurrency, distinct from the `40001` serialization-failure case it already caught) as a CAS loss, not an unhandled 500; **provider state integrity** — `RedFlagProvider` now distinguishes loading/error/empty (a failed refresh preserves last-good rows instead of clearing them), guards against out-of-order overlapping refresh responses via a generation counter, and disables `create()` until the first successful load. Round 6 (independent review of round 5 + reconciliation with TASK-008 RM4's now-landed migration `0015`): a fresh review verified all 11 round-5 items and flagged 2 non-blocking follow-ups (a docs/BUGS.md accuracy correction; `RedFlagProvider`'s `error` state gained a `retryLoad()` affordance), both fixed. RM4 landed with its own relation-proposal privacy mechanism (`assertRelationshipProposalOwner`, a new `action.listHistory` endpoint, `privateOwnerUserId`-based store-level filtering) — reconciled surgically: TASK-010's own `inputs.visibility === "private"` red-flag privacy check now composes alongside RM4's relation-specific check rather than duplicating it, and the underlying store-level filter is being widened (not replaced) to cover both. Re-verified: core 426, DB 121, API 211 (54 red-flag/security tests across all rounds), JobPilot 94, DealPilot 72, Commons 22, integrations-google 35, web 64 tests, full monorepo build (20/20) and typecheck (37/37) clean, full serialized test run 36/37 green (the one failure is `@bridge/sensors`'s pre-existing, already-documented, unrelated coverage-floor gap), lint clean on every session-touched file (2 pre-existing unrelated repo-wide findings untouched), no-dummy-runtime clean. A pre-existing, unrelated dummy-data instance (`JobPilotApplicationDetail.tsx`'s unrouted `BCG_APPLICATION` fixture) was discovered and logged to `docs/dummy.md`, and its red-flag wiring removed since the page is unreachable. Outstanding before this task can close: live desktop+375px browser evidence of the actual hover/focus/click/touch interaction, and the post-RM4 migration (owner-aware RLS, DB-backed lineage revision, JobPilot flag backfill) now in progress. Round 7 (post-TASK-008-RM4, [round-7 output](../outputs/2026-07-18-task010-round7-post-rm4-migration.md)): merged `origin/main` (`590cca6`) and reconciled surgically with RM4's own relation-proposal privacy mechanism, finding and fixing a genuine post-merge leak (RM4's new endpoints would have exposed red-flag proposals to every workspace member — widened the shared private-proposal filter to cover both privacy markers) and a merge-tool duplicate-member bug. Implemented the reserved migration `0016_new_ink`: JobPilot flag backfill+CHECK constraint; owner-aware RLS for `memories` (mirroring the existing app-side scope split); `memories.lineage_revision` WIRED end-to-end (atomically allocated per-lineage inside `casSupersede`'s own CAS transaction in both adapters; `redFlag.history` now orders by it). `ledger`'s own RLS deliberately left unchanged, matching RM4's own considered precedent (no caller-identity parameter on its read ports, broad call surface) — reported as an open, scoped gap, not silently deferred. Also fixed two build-tooling bugs surfaced by full-build verification: RM4's TASKS.md reformat had silently broken the Task Manager's `pending-work` generator (now fixed + regenerated, 22 records); a commons test wrote temp fixtures inside the repo tree instead of the OS tmpdir (fixed). Re-verified: core 430, DB 143, API 214, web 64, commons 22 tests, full monorepo typecheck (22/22)/build (23/23) clean, lint clean (2 pre-existing unrelated findings untouched), no-dummy-runtime clean, migration 0016 fresh+upgrade/no-drift tests pass. Outstanding unchanged: live desktop+375px browser evidence; additionally now open: `ledger`'s own RLS (deliberately deferred) and a dedicated two-member RLS test for the new `memories` policy. A fresh independent review verdict was **merge-ready** with 2 non-blocking findings, both fixed same day: a dangling `docs/BUGS.md` cross-reference ("see the NEW entry below" pointing to a never-added entry) corrected; and the flagged missing two-member RLS test for `memories` added (`packages/db/test/rls.test.ts`, using the existing `bridge_rls_member` restricted-role pattern) — proves public/workspace/team/private/restricted scope visibility AND that UPDATE/DELETE (not just SELECT) are RLS-narrowed by real ownership under Postgres itself, re-verified: DB 144/144 (+1).

Required Skill selected only Internal Strategist; ambiguous/no-match required Human assignment; Capability Builder was never default.
- Gap audit + TM3 slice 1 (2026-08-08, ADR-196/AP-116): an audit against `taskmanager-module-plan-2026-07.md` found the TM3 "planning intelligence" slice DECLARED but not built — all 18 `task-manager.*` Skill ids were registered manifests whose `run()` fell through to `return { proposedOutput: inputs }` (an echo; only `create-task` had behaviour), and `draftTaskCreate`'s `impact_fit` payload was the single boolean `requiresResequenceReview: true`. Closed for three of them: new pure `packages/core/src/task-planning.ts` (`findDuplicateTasks` — duplicate bar 0.6 score / 3 shared terms, deliberately HIGHER than parent suggestion's 0.34/2; `proposeQueueSequence`; `analyzeTaskImpactFit`), the `impact_fit` proposal now carries real findings with pre-existing keys preserved, the flag became honest (`resequence.changed`), and `wiring.ts`'s `runTaskPlanningSkill` makes the three Skills execute instead of echo. Deterministic by choice (ADR-183/ADR-177 rule: runs on every create, must show its terms, must degrade to "nothing found" not a guess); Skills stay pure (queue is authorized input; a missing queue THROWS rather than faking a clean result). Verified: core `task-planning.test.ts` 5/5 seen RED first (the `draftTaskCreate` assertion failed on "duplicates must reach the proposal" before the wiring), api `task-planning-skills.test.ts` 2/2, turbo typecheck+test:coverage+build 70/72 green — the 2 non-green were `@bridge/db` `spawnSync ... ETIMEDOUT` under load, re-run clean in isolation (637ms, pass), and nothing in this change touches schema or migrations.
- TM3 slice 2 — the four model-backed Skills and the Playbooks behind them (2026-08-08, ADR-197/AP-117): these were one gap, not two. The plan calls the four Skills "methodology-parameterized" and `TASK_MANAGER_PLAYBOOKS` was five ids with no content, so there was nothing to parameterize them WITH and nothing consuming the Playbooks. New `packages/core/src/task-playbooks.ts` gives all five Playbooks real content (methodology, intent, the technique's questions, system-prompt guidance, supported Skills) and implements `frameGoalOutcomes` / `generateCandidateTasks` / `runPremortem` / `decomposeTask`; `TASK_MANAGER_PLAYBOOKS` is now derived from that content so the two cannot drift; `wiring.ts`'s `runTaskAuthoringSkill` makes the four execute instead of echo, declared `authority_bearing` because a Skill that calls a model is not pure data. Model-backed here (unlike slice 1's three) because these are invoked deliberately, produce prose a human edits, and never decide placement. **With no model configured they return the Playbook's questions with `source: "playbook_scaffold"`, zero drafted items, and a note saying why** — a bare empty list would be indistinguishable from "the methodology found nothing"; the same degrade catches prose, malformed JSON, and entries failing validation. Local Plane only (`planeDefault: "local"` makes the router fail rather than fall through to cloud, matching the manifests' own `plane: "local"`), resolved per call because the desktop managed model only turns healthy ~30s after boot. Structure the model may NOT author: child dot-paths computed from the parent and highest direct sibling, `status: "candidate"` pinned, no outcome ids, at most one north star, missing `parentPath` throws. Verified: core 563/563 with the four load-bearing assertions mutation-checked individually, api 442 pass / 1 skipped / 0 fail with the four new registry tests seen RED against the restored echo, turbo typecheck 44/44, `check:vocabulary` clean for every file in this change set.
- TM3/TM4 slice 3 — the catalog stops echoing, and two Automations get runtime bindings (2026-08-08, ADR-198/AP-118): new `packages/core/src/task-execution.ts` implements the four Skills with no logic anywhere (`verifyTaskEvidence`, `synthesizeProgress`, `scaffoldHabits`, `scanForOpportunities`); `exit-test-authoring` joins the model-backed Playbook Skills under the existing `measurable-review` Playbook, keeping the plan's v1 cap of 5; `runTaskExecutionSkill` binds those plus five whose logic already existed in core and had simply never been bound to a Skill id. **The echo fallback is now a THROW** — a registered `task-manager.*` id with no implementation fails loudly rather than looking like a working Skill, which is the mechanism that let TM3 ship declared-but-not-built. Two procedures put Skills on a real path for the first time: `taskManager.runPlanningPlaybook` (new `planning-playbook` Automation, human-triggered) and `taskManager.runOpportunityScan` (`proactive-scan-cadence`, declared since TM0 with no runtime id), both attributable Internal Strategist Runs halting at `pending_review`. Manifest `1.1.0`→`1.2.0`. Where the Skills stop: `verifyTaskEvidence` proposes a verification with **no `verifiedBy`** (the only honest verifier is whoever accepts it; filling it would put an Agent's name on a verification record) and never claims the evidence satisfies the exit test; `scaffoldHabits` refuses a non-goal instead of inventing a rhythm; `scanForOpportunities` states it read the queue, not cross-Module Signals. Internal Strategist gains exactly the six Skills a procedure invokes, capability scope unchanged, list shared from `@bridge/db` so the two durability seeds cannot drift. Verified: core 574/574 (invariants mutation-checked), api 447 pass / 1 skipped / 0 fail including a catalog-wide no-echo test over all 18 Skills seen RED against a restored echo, db 234/234, typecheck 44/44.
- TM3/TM4 slice 4 — approval materializes, and the model call is governed (2026-08-08, ADR-199/AP-119): new pure `packages/core/src/task-materialize.ts` (`applyApprovedPlanningProposal`) called by BOTH `decideProposal` implementations so the two durability backends cannot materialize differently; `TaskProposalKind` already had `candidate` and `kind` is a `text` column so **no migration**; the procedures now share one id between the pipeline proposal and the Task-Manager `candidate` row, so a single human decision resolves both. `RunCtx` gains an optional `modelProvider`, letting `runPlanningPlaybook` hand the Skill a `createGovernedModelProvider`-wrapped LOCAL provider and return a real `modelReceiptLedgerId` — plane unchanged, because governance is not consent. The limits are the design: generated Tasks land as `candidate` (approved means "worth having", not "start these"); dot-paths are RECOMPUTED against the current queue so a queue that moved between draft and approval cannot put two Tasks at one address; an approved pre-mortem writes nothing on purpose; approved outcomes never arrive as north star; each scan candidate lands under the Task its own finding named, and a finding whose source row is gone is dropped; generated Tasks are owned by the APPROVER, never the drafting Agent. Verified: core 583/583 with the candidate-status, north-star and finding-provenance invariants mutation-checked; api 450 pass / 1 skipped / 0 fail including an end-to-end test where a local model double drafts, the governed Run halts, approval creates the child Tasks and a veto writes nothing — seen RED twice (materialize branch disabled; governed provider dropped); db 234/234; typecheck 44/44.
- TM3/TM4 slice 5 — a reviewer may correct a planning draft, not only accept or reject it (2026-08-08, ADR-200/AP-120): `candidate` joins `projection_reconcile` as an editable proposal kind, so one bad child title in an eight-child decomposition no longer costs the whole draft. New pure `mergeEditedPlanningPayload` (`packages/core/src/task-materialize.ts`) makes the edit an allow-list MERGE over the ONE content key the staged kind materializes from — `kind`, the run id, the model receipt and the Playbook's questions carry over verbatim, and `kind` is absent from the wire schema entirely because it selects which branch of the materializer runs (an edit able to carry a new one could turn a reviewed pre-mortem into an unreviewed decomposition). Three layers of validation, because a human's edited text is exactly as untrusted as a model's output: `.strict()` at the API boundary, the merge against the kind, then the materializer per entry. Two deliberate refusals: an edit that materializes nothing is rejected (an approval that writes nothing is a veto wearing an approval's clothes), and a pre-mortem refuses an edit outright (approving one writes nothing by design). The Agent's original draft is preserved on the row under `agentDraft`, because the queue-side row is where a reviewer looks. Two smaller gaps behind it also closed: new `taskManager.proposal` read query (nothing could fetch a Task-side draft to show a reviewer, so an edit surface had no read surface), and a decided `candidate` proposal's Run is now finished with the decision and result rather than ending at "a draft was produced". `archive_sweep`/restructure stay approve-or-veto on purpose — they are computed plans over specific rows and versions checked for staleness at decision time, so an edited one is a different unchecked plan, not a corrected draft. Verified: core 588/588 with all four new invariants mutation-checked one at a time; api 453 total / 452 pass / 1 skipped / 0 fail with the two new API tests seen RED twice; db 234/234.
- TM3/TM4 slice 6 — Chief of Staff gets a runtime Agent identity, and its two cadence Automations run (2026-08-08, ADR-201/AP-121): the blocker ADR-198 recorded and correctly refused to work around. Four Automations and three Skills are declared under Chief of Staff by ADR-107's design and NONE could run, because `resolveModuleAgentRuntimeId` resolved only Internal Strategist and Governance. An Automation starts an Agent Run and only an attributable Agent may invoke a Skill, so an owner with no runtime identity owns nothing that can run — the ADR-107 ownership split was decorative. CoS now has a physical governed identity with a deliberately narrow scope (no `record:archive`: it coordinates and reports, Governance archives) and exactly one allow-listed Skill, the only one a CoS procedure invokes. `standup-brief` and `stale-task-review` gain runtime ids and one procedure `taskManager.runQueueBrief`; both REPORT and stop — no Task-Manager proposal, step action `read`, no proposed status, because making a Human approve being told about their own Tasks is theatre and what a rotting Task needs is a judgement. One Skill behind both: `progress-synthesis`'s `stalled` list IS the staleness question over a longer window. `evaluateTaskGuards` gains the `stale_task` finding it never had, sharing `isStalenessEligible` with `synthesizeProgress` — a `candidate` and a `parked` Task are SUPPOSED to sit untouched, `blocked` is not; staleness is skipped rather than guessed with no clock; the WIP limit becomes a parameter. Manifest `1.2.0`→`1.3.0`, and its 1.2.0 comment corrected (it wrongly claimed `standup-brief` was bound under ADR-198). Verified: core 592/592, api 454 pass / 1 skipped / 0 fail, db 234/234, turbo 48/48; two mutation attempts SURVIVED first and each exposed a real defect — the guard inlined a check instead of calling the shared helper, and an API-level assertion was vacuous because every row created through the API is new.
- TM3/TM4 slice 7 — the queue guard and the approval gate become Skills (2026-08-08, ADR-202/AP-122): four Governance Automations declared since TM0 had nothing to run, because the computations they need (`evaluateTaskGuards`, `classifyTaskChangeBand`, `calibratedTaskChangeDecision`) existed as core code with NO Skill id — `evaluateTaskGuards` was reachable only as read-only data hanging off the `projection` query. Two new Governance Skills (`queue-guard`, `change-gate`), runtime ids for all four Automations, procedures `runQueueGuard`/`runChangeGate`, shared `GOVERNANCE_ALLOWED_SKILLS`. **The gate now calibrates on REAL decision history** — a gate that trusts the caller's account of its own track record is not a gate; it counts approve/veto rows matching `skill === "task-manager.change-gate"` and the change kind. Two Skills not one (queue state and change permission are different questions), and each Automation reports only its own finding kind (a WIP breach and an unverified `done` have different remedies). Least privilege exposed a real mismatch: both Skills only read, the first run was REJECTED because Governance has no `record:write`, and the fix was narrowing the Skills rather than widening the Agent. Recorded finding: `unverified-done-challenger` has no reachable path through `transition` — the store already refuses `done` without verification — so it covers rows arriving by projection reconcile, which is why it exists. Manifest `1.3.0`→`1.4.0`. Verified: api 456 pass / 1 skipped / 0 fail with both new tests mutation-checked; db 234/234; turbo 48/48.
- TM3/TM4 slice 8 — the four Internal Strategist Automations get runtime bindings (2026-08-08, ADR-203/AP-123): three of them already raised a governed proposal that halted for review, so the gap was never governance — it was **attribution**. Each proposal's actor was `ctx.identity` (the Human who triggered it) with `skill: KERNEL_PASSTHROUGH_SKILL`, so the wiki's own load-bearing rule ("every Task/Goal create triggers Internal Strategist impact-fit-analysis") described an analysis that was real but had no Agent Run behind it and no Skill invocation to point at. One shared `runTaskManagerAgentAutomation` helper now runs each as its owning Agent through the executor, invoking the real Skill and sharing the proposal id with the queue-side row. This is MORE governance, not a convenience: an Agent actor passes the allow-list and capability scope a Human bypasses, which is why the grants had to be written down. `target-change-reopen-prompt` needed no Skill of its own — "should this reopen because what we want changed" IS "does this still fit" — so `impact-fit-analysis` supplies the reasoning attached to the prompt. `goal-review-cadence` stays Internal Strategist's even though `queue-guard` is Governance-owned by default, because whether a goal is due for review is a planning question (ADR-107's split applied to a guard). Manifest `1.4.0`→`1.5.0`. Verified: api 459 pass / 1 skipped / 0 fail with both attribution claims mutation-checked (kernel-passthrough revert failed 4, wrong-Agent revert failed 1); core 592/592; db 234/234; turbo 48/48.
- TM3/TM4 slice 9 — Task dependency Relations (2026-08-08, ADR-204/AP-124, **migration 0039**): the residual every slice since ADR-196 recorded in the same words. `proposeQueueSequence` honoured the `blocked` STATUS, which records that someone believed a Task was blocked but not by WHAT — so nothing could ever tell them it had stopped being true, and `dependency-unblock-notifier` had no edges whose clearing it could notice. New `task_dependencies` table, pure `task-dependencies.ts`, both stores, dependency-aware sequencing and impact-fit, a Chief of Staff `dependency-analysis` Skill, and the notifier bound. **ONE edge kind** — `blocked_by` is the same edge read from the other end, and storing both directions would let them disagree. **A cycle is refused at write time** (409, not 500 — the request was well-formed and the answer is no), with a CHECK constraint for the one-hop case and `FOR UPDATE` on the edge set so two concurrent edges cannot close a loop together. A blocker that is done, **abandoned**, or gone no longer blocks — leaving a dependent waiting on work nobody will do is the rot the edge exists to expose. "Unblocked" means the wait ended, not that there never was one. **The notifier notifies**: a blocker landing does not make the dependent Task started. Migration is purely additive with no backfill, so `DROP TABLE` reverses it exactly; composite FKs make a cross-tenant edge inexpressible. Manifest `1.5.0`→`1.6.0`. Verified: core 597/597 with all four invariants mutation-checked individually; api 461 pass / 1 skipped / 0 fail; db 234/234 including the migration no-op check; turbo 48/48. Three pins updated deliberately (migration head, manifest version, catalog-size floor 18→21).
- TM5 slice 10 — the queue reaches Second Brain (2026-08-08, ADR-205/AP-125): three things were wrong and only one was written down. **Second Brain had no Tasks** — ADR-110 settled that Second Brain IS Graph view at `scope: full`, and `graph.full` composed Records/People/Communities/Events/Modules/Agents while the one Database the user works out of daily was invisible. **Graph view was structurally impossible on the Task Page** — `computeEligibleKinds` needs a non-parent relation and the Task Database had only `parentTaskId`, so this had to follow ADR-204's `depends_on` rather than precede it; Calendar and Tree were already eligible. **Nine Automation manifest entries were lying** — `automationId` came from a four-branch ternary listing the ADR-198 bindings, so every binding added since claimed no runtime Automation stood behind it, which is exactly the declared-not-built signal this workstream exists to make trustworthy; it now derives from the one resolver that owns runtime ids. Both Task edge kinds are composed because they are different questions (where work SITS vs what it WAITS ON); the queue honours the same node cap and drops closed work first, with `hasMore` accounting for the rest; `dependsOn` is read-only in the grid because an edge can close a cycle and the refusal must not hide behind a reverting cell. Manifest `1.6.0`→`1.7.0`. Verified: api 462 pass / 1 skipped / 0 fail with the graph test mutation-checked twice; web 134/135 (one pre-existing avatar failure, reproduced at base); turbo 48/48.
- TM6 slice 11 — Playbooks declared, Commons entry made findable (2026-08-08, ADR-206/AP-126): TM6's deliverable reads "Module + Playbooks as signed Commons **capabilities**", and this deliberately diverges. ADR-180 established that a capability is the thing that is GOVERNED — permissions and a trust lifecycle — which is why `view` stopped being one. A Playbook holds no permissions; the Skills it names hold them all, and giving a Playbook permissions would create a second place authority could widen unnoticed. So `ModulePlaybookBinding` is a Module-surface declaration, validated by `parseModuleManifest` (a Playbook must name ≥1 Skill, and every Skill it names must be one this Module declares) and derived from `TASK_PLAYBOOKS` rather than restated. It still has to be declared: a manifest omitting it hands a fresh Organization five methodologies its own manifest never mentioned, so nothing installed could be audited against what arrived. The Commons entry gains capability tags plus three `need:` tags — an entry tagged only `built-in` + kind is present but unfindable. The no-personal-data audit is over the SERIALIZED entry (e-mail markers, pilot identity, and any UUID at all), because a leak arrives in whatever field nobody thought to check. Manifest `1.7.0`→`1.8.0`. Verified: manifests 10/10 with both new tests mutation-checked; api 462 pass / 1 skipped / 0 fail; core 597/597; turbo 48/48.
- TM4 slice 12 — the routing DECISION surface, and the last unbound Automation (2026-08-08, ADR-207/AP-128): `agent-task-routing-on-assign` stayed last through eight slices that bound thirteen others, and every one of them recorded the right reason: it needed a decision SURFACE, not a wiring. `routeTaskByRequiredSkill` has existed since TM0 and `taskManager.route` exposed it — as a **query**. It answered "who is eligible for this Task" and nothing could act on the answer, because no write path set `assignedAgentId` after creation; `create` accepted one and nothing ever changed it. Binding the Automation to that computation would have produced an attributable Run that decides nothing, which is the declared-not-built shape this workstream exists to end. Now: `route` is a stageable proposal kind, pure `applyApprovedRoutingProposal` is called by BOTH stores, and `taskManager.assign` runs routing as a **Chief of Staff** Run (ADR-107 owns routing) and the band/calibration as a **Governance** one (ADR-202 owns the gate) — two Runs, because collapsing them puts the permission question inside the Agent that benefits from the answer. The candidate set is `TASK_ROUTING_CANDIDATE_AGENTS`, resolved server-side: `route`'s caller-supplied list is harmless for a what-if, but on a path that WRITES a caller naming one Agent manufactures the unambiguous result, and "the only candidate offered" becoming "the eligible Agent" is the default-by-omission ADR-107 forbids. **`human_assignment_required` stages nothing** — no proposal, no approve button, because offering one invites someone to approve an assignment nobody computed. Routing to another Module's Skill is `crossesModule`, so it can never be minor and always stops for a Human. The calibrated `auto_apply` branch is HONOURED rather than merely computed, through the same two steps a clicked approval takes, with `calibrated: true` and the counts it was granted on — a gate whose permissive branch never fires is decorative. An approved routing sets `assignedAgentId` and stops: authority to run a Task is not the work having started. Two staleness refusals named separately (the Task's version, and its `requiredSkillId` no longer being the one the Agent was found eligible FOR — the failure a reviewer causes themselves by editing the Task while its routing sits in review). `agent-task-routing` narrows to `record:read`; Chief of Staff gains it, the grant ADR-201 deliberately withheld until something called it. `runChangeGate` and `assign` now share one `runTaskChangeGate` helper — a gate with two copies is two gates. Manifest `1.8.0`→`1.9.0`, no migration. Verified: core 19/19 in `task-materialize` with three invariants mutation-checked individually; api 22/22 in `task-manager-automations` with attribution and the staging refusal mutation-checked; turbo 72/72.
- Slice 13 — three governed decisions become human-reachable (2026-08-09, ADR-208/AP-129): every slice from ADR-200 onward recorded the same residual in the same words — no UI reaches the planning edit or creates a dependency edge — and ADR-207 added the routing assignment to that list. For a decision surface that is not a partial state: the decision does not exist for the person whose queue it is. The Task Record Page now carries all three. **The rules live in a pure `task-proposal-review.ts`, not in the component** — those are the parts that can be WRONG, and this repository's own 2026-08-03 test audit deleted ten source-grep suites precisely because a test that cannot import behaviour cannot fail on it; the Node runner strips types but does not transform JSX, so putting the logic where it CAN be asserted is what makes the surface testable at all. `EDITABLE_CONTENT_KEY` is IMPORTED from the materializer rather than copied: a private copy renders an empty list the moment core adds a kind, and the reviewer reads "this plan proposes nothing" about a plan that proposes plenty. Only the fields a kind materializes from are shown and sent — the wire schema is `.strict()`, and showing a field the kind ignores invites an edit that is silently dropped. A refusal is DATA, not an exception: a pre-mortem stays readable with the API's own sentence and no edit button, because the one kind with nothing to edit must not be the one kind nobody can see. **The live check earned its keep**: running the real API against the real Page showed a proposal labelled "Playbook scaffold" with no methodology, no questions and no explanation — this module read an invented `questions` key while the payload uses `prompts`, and the unit test asserted the same invented key, so it passed. A test written against a shape you assumed cannot fail on the shape being wrong. Fixed to read `prompts` and surface the Skill's `note` and the Playbook's `methodology`; the test now asserts a payload captured verbatim from a live Run. Verified live: a routing proposal approved through the UI wrote `assignedAgentId` and left the status untouched, a dependency edge was created, and a cycle was refused with its reason rendered as a sentence. Web logic 9/9 with three invariants mutation-checked. No API change, no migration, no manifest bump.
- Slice 14 — TM6's remaining deliverables, and a lie in the agent ledger (2026-08-09, ADR-209/AP-130): TM6 named a **per-repo agent-ledger template** and an exit criterion of a fresh Organization **installing** from Commons; ADR-206 delivered the packaging and audited what WOULD be published, without writing a template or installing anything. Found on the way in: the projection's `- Dependencies:` field was **hardcoded to `none`** from TM0 — true while the schema had no edges, and a lie to the ledger's only reader from the moment ADR-204 shipped them. Same class as the nine Automations claiming no runtime id (ADR-205): a value frozen at the moment before the thing it describes existed. It now names live blockers by path and title, and distinguishes **`not read`** from `none` — a caller that did not load the edges has not established that a Task waits on nothing, and reporting the absence of a query as the absence of a dependency is the same lie in a quieter voice. Only LIVE blockers are listed (a done or abandoned blocker has stopped blocking) with the cleared count reported separately, because "nothing is in the way now" and "nothing was ever in the way" are different facts. The edges are projected CONTENT so they enter the drift hash — correct, and why every emit site was threaded; the Drizzle store reads them through its own transaction rather than opening a second one outside the row locks. New `emitAgentLedgerTemplate` writes `AGENTS.md` beside the projection in the same operation: the two are one artifact, and every fact in it is DERIVED from the constant that enforces it, because a template that drifts from its system teaches a contract the server then refuses. TM6's exit criterion is now PERFORMED — a real install (propose → Human decision → promote), TM1's prototype test on the installed Module, then the no-personal-data audit over what Commons holds AFTER the install and real Task work. **Three real behaviours it found**: a root Module install refuses a `needId` (`need:` tags are for discovery, not attachment); `installPropose` resolves an existing row for a version already held; and installing this Module **stops for a Human** rather than auto-activating — governance working, now asserted rather than assumed. Honest scope: "fresh Organization" is a fresh STATE inside the one permitted pilot id, so the tenant boundary is not exercised. Verified: core 9/9 with three invariants mutation-checked (restoring the hardcoded `none` failed 6); api commons 18/18, task-manager 2/2; turbo 72/72; `check:agent-context` 0; vocabulary clean for this change set. One pre-existing db test CORRECTED rather than loosened — it computed its before-hash without edges, the exact inconsistency this makes impossible in production. No migration, no manifest bump.
- Slice 15 — the Run/Task anchor, directive capture, and a parser bug that hid seven active Tasks (2026-09-01, ADR-265/266, AP-172): asked whether every Avatar Task and every agentic Run is routed through Task Manager, the audit returned three different answers rather than one. Agent Runs were ALREADY routed — `pipeline.propose` rejects a governed Skill without a `goalTaskRef`, and `child_agent_runs`/`research_runs` both carry `task_id` NOT NULL. A second gap I reported — unmanifested Skills bypassing the anchor — proved UNREACHABLE for an Agent actor (authority Layer 0's `agentFloorDeny` fires before the AGS1 branch that exempts them), and was withdrawn rather than closed with code protecting nothing. The real gap was `automation_runs`, which had no Task column at all: the anchor lived only on the Automation STEP, one mutable row shared by every Run, so "which Task did this Run advance" could only be answered as "whatever the step says today". Migration 0044 adds `task_id`, nullable and composite-FK'd, projected at Run start from the first step carrying a `goalTaskRef` — nullable because a backfill would assert an anchor the ledger never witnessed. For capture, the plumbing was never missing: `stageChatTaskProposal`, thread anchoring and the deterministic parent suggestion all worked, and ONE sentence in the Chat output contract recognized only an explicit "create a Task" request, so ordinary directives produced an answer and no Record. That sentence now captures any instruction. **The human review card is deliberately kept**: minting without review would put an Agent in the approver seat `AGENT_FLOOR_MUTATIONS` denies. Then, asked to see the queue: `parseCanonicalTasks` read the WHOLE `- Status:` line, so the 16 tasks that annotate their status (`in_progress (LANDED 2026-08-30, ADR-262; the invite half is NOT met)`) matched no known status — the projection called them `open` and the always-loaded index dropped seven active Tasks, six in progress, for exactly the reason that made them worth annotating. The status is now a token plus a verbatim `statusNote`, and `activeTasksBytes` rises 4_096 → 4_608 because the old ceiling was the size of an index that was missing them. New `assignHorizonHierarchy` builds the tree from `Horizon` — the one grouping each record actually declares — into 7 Goal nodes over 87 tasks, 49 completed / 14 in progress / 21 pending / 3 blocked, none unfiled; task-to-task parentage was NOT invented and `Dependencies` stays a sibling edge (ADR-204), not parenthood. Verified: `task-doc-parser` 9/9 with both new cases seen FAILING against `git show HEAD` first; full workspace build 34/34 with no TS errors; `@bridge/core` green under `TZ=UTC`; `@bridge/db` 239/239 (the `migration-metadata` head-pin advanced 0043 -> 0044, and its `drizzle-kit generate` no-op assertion is what PROVES the hand-written 0044 snapshot matches `schema.ts` — the composite FK is absent from both, by design); api `task-manager` + `task-manager-automations` + `chat` 45/45; `check:agent-context` green at the new budget.
- Slice 16 — the ledger becomes real Task Records (2026-09-01, ADR-267, AP-173): slice 15 left a correct projection with no consumer. Three existing paths looked like they should carry it and none does — `create` in a loop forces every Task after the first to `candidate` and stages 93 impact-fit proposals, asking a Human to re-approve work finished weeks ago; `proposeProjectionReconcile` demands the content byte-equal the Module folder's own `tasks.md` and throws on any id it does not already hold, so it is a round-trip and not an importer; a CLI would need its own wiring, auth, and a second place that knows a Task Record's shape. So: a fourth path at three layers — pure `draftCanonicalLedgerImport`, `TaskManagerStore.importCanonicalLedger` in ONE transaction on both implementations (a half-imported tree is a queue whose paths disagree with its source and nothing downstream can tell which half is real), and one procedure. **The rule that separates it from intake is a single line: an imported Task lands at the status the ledger states.** Everything else intake guarantees is kept — dot-paths recomputed rather than trusted, and the non-goal `in_progress` exit-test invariant still enforced, with any entry that would breach it admitted as `pending` and NAMED in the result rather than quietly downgraded. Idempotence replaces intake review as the guard: `idempotentUuid(org:canonical-ledger:<recordId>)`, so pressing the button twice re-states statuses and mints nothing — asserted, and mutation-checked by deleting the existing-row lookup, which fails it. The payload is the committed PROJECTION rather than the Markdown: docs/TASKS.md is a repository file the API cannot reach in a packaged desktop build, `pending-work.generated.json` already ships in the web bundle, and a second server-side parser could disagree with the first. That also revives `pending-work.ts`, dead since `/pending-work` began redirecting to `/task-manager`. ## End-to-end runtime taint tracking
- ID: TASK-015
- Status: done
- Priority: P3
- Horizon: Hardening
- Outcome: Trust labels propagate monotonically across retrieval, prompts, models, Skills, Actions, Events, Results, Files, storage, serialization, caches, queues, retries, and governed declassification.
- Prototype test: A tainted source traverses an Agent/Skill/Automation path without losing or weakening its label; unknown labels fail closed; sink traces explain the decision; deterministic or Human-approved declassification is audited.
- Scope: docs/raw/learning-agent-roadmap-2026-07.md RT0–RT4; docs/wiki/roadmap.md
- Evidence: AP-070/ADR-142 close RT0–RT4. `platform/packages/core/src/taint.ts` is the canonical v1 lattice/envelope/source-sink/declassification/replay contract; `TrustOrigin` remains only an explicit fail-closed v0 compatibility projection. Migration `0029_task015_runtime_taint` backfills Ledger, Memory, Event/Signal projection, File, Automation Run, and child Run labels; unlabeled/malformed rows become `UNKNOWN_LABEL`; immutable sink/declassification tables use forced Organization RLS. Server-owned Human/system/sensor/Google/web/MCP/signed-Commons edges derive labels; model, Skill, pipeline, Automation, child Run, Result, Memory, cache/queue/retry, Event, File, and restart paths join/persist them. Approval UI shows untrusted/unknown warning plus source trace. Targeted proof: 175 core/DB checks (one taint-coupled Memory regression fixed), 81 local/model/net-guard/sensor checks, 19 Google checks, 17 web-research/Agent checks, 8 approval/declassification checks, 38 culture-research/restart checks, migration fresh+upgrade+replay+RLS, one genuine file-backed A→B restart, and 3 UI trace checks; affected builds/typechecks/lint/vocabulary/no-dummy pass. Source `da26f465187230b09f758b6f3e88b52af050381e` landed through PR #46 at merge `5857cb95d98c53928103df801cfc4ff30a73e8e3`; source ancestry and `origin/main` were verified. GitHub Actions remain payment-blocked; no CI success is claimed.
- Requests: runtime taint directive 2026-07-14
- Approval: AP-020, AP-029, and AP-070 applied
- Dependencies: TASK-007 (done); TASK-012 (done)
- Verification: Prototype PASS — a web-derived label traverses Skill→pipeline→Ledger→sink trace and file-backed process A→B without weakening; unknown/malformed history is quarantined; deterministic validator and accountable authenticated Human declassification are immutable; instruction-bearing injection retries produce zero Events/Actions; Approval detail exposes prompt-free provenance. One bounded changed-scope review found nine sink/audit/Plane/legacy/web/Automation/model/uniqueness/declassification defects; all nine were fixed with direct regressions before closure. Exact CDP emulation reports `innerWidth=375`, `scrollWidth=375`; desktop and 375px Approval renders are retained as session artifacts.

## Inference cost optimization: prompt caching + model tiering
- ID: TASK-022
- Status: done
- Priority: P2
- Horizon: Core Modules
- Outcome: ModelProvider calls use Anthropic prompt caching on the stable prefix, model selection routes by cost/capability tier (cheap/default/reasoning) instead of first-registered-provider, and complete() returns usage token counts enabling cost receipts — all preserving the local-plane-never-falls-to-cloud rule.
- Prototype test: A repeated CoS turn shows non-zero cache_read_input_tokens on the second call; CoS intent classification runs on the cheap tier while a reasoning-tagged call runs on a higher tier; complete() usage is logged for at least one call site.
- Scope: platform/packages/models/src/anthropic-provider.ts; platform/packages/models/src/router.ts; platform/packages/core/src/ports.ts; platform/packages/core/src/run-context.ts; platform/packages/core/src/chief-of-staff.ts; platform/apps/api/src/router.ts (~4573); docs/raw/optimizations-memory-vm-dealpilot-plan-2026-07.md (phase 1 slice)
- Evidence: outputs/2026-07-17-llm-inference-optimization-audit.md (zero runtime prompt optimization confirmed: no cache_control, no batching, no tiering, model = providers[0]); outputs/2026-07-18-task-022-inference-cost-optimization.md implements tier/Plane/health routing, stable Anthropic system-prefix caching, exact provider usage/model identity, bounded prompt-free Run/Agent/Organization receipts, Local-default CoS inference, and explicit public-data confirmation before cloud egress. Deterministic affected checks and independent correctness/security review pass. Status is `blocked` under AP-067: exact unblock requires an already-authorized Anthropic credential plus explicit live-test spend authorization, then two sequential real public-safe CoS turns proving non-zero `cache_read_input_tokens` on the second persisted receipt. No credential, spend authorization, or live provider claim exists in this environment.
- Closure (2026-07-31, AP-092): implementation and independent review were already complete; the only outstanding clause was a live Anthropic cache-hit number, which moved to TASK-030 PT-1. The harness is provider-agnostic by construction — `ModelProvider` carries `tiers`/`models`/`pricing`/`routingHealth`/`plane`, `MODEL_TIERS` is cheap|default|reasoning, and four adapters implement it (Anthropic, Groq, Ollama, llama.cpp). Tier routing, cache wire-format parsing, and receipt construction are all proven WITHOUT a credential. `cache_read_input_tokens` stays Anthropic-only because it is a provider feature behind an agnostic port, not a design gap.
- Requests: LLM prompt/infra optimization audit directive 2026-07-17
- Approval: AP-038 and AP-067 applied
- Dependencies: none — the live Anthropic cache-hit proof moved to TASK-030 PT-1; tier routing, cache wire format, and receipts are already proven deterministically without a credential

## WhatsApp Module with governed contact extraction
- ID: TASK-029
- Status: in_progress
- Priority: P2
- Estimate: 5d
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: Clicking WhatsApp in the left nav opens the owner's own WhatsApp Web session inside the Bridge desktop shell, with a sibling Tools Page whose Contact Extractor turns individual contacts into a Local Plane person list and selected groups into Communities with policy-bounded participant membership. v1 is read-only, enforced by a fixed operation allowlist in the desktop shell rather than by convention; raw capture and phone numbers never leave the Local Plane.
- Prototype test: From a cold app launch, the WhatsApp nav entry opens a linked session without a new QR scan; the Contact Extractor stages at least one real contact into the local WhatsApp list; the staged Person carries a phone number that is present in the Local Plane and absent from cloud canonical; a `@lid` contact is staged with a name and NO fabricated number; and `whatsapp_extract_start("send_message")` is refused by the shell.
- Scope: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-01-whatsapp-module-design.md; docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-whatsapp-module.md
- Evidence: Live WKWebView spike 2026-08-01 established three load-bearing facts — WhatsApp Web serves an unsupported-browser wall to WKWebView's default user agent and needs an explicit Safari `Version/` token; a linked session DOES survive a full process restart despite `aquire-persistent-storage-denied`; and neither `WPP.isReady` nor an authenticated connection means the client can answer a read (an UNLAUNCHED socket fails with "sendIq called before startComms"). Real-account extraction returned 8,384 contacts, 817 groups, 35,298 unique participants and 54,245 memberships, which exposed two defects in committed code: WhatsApp Linked IDs were being converted into fabricated phone numbers for 4,203 of 8,384 contacts, and guarding the id alone was insufficient because the extraction layer had already laundered LID digits into a `phone` field. Both fixed with regression tests. Desktop Rust 6/6, module package 47/47, Google intake 5/5, Local Plane list test passing.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-01 (WhatsApp module with WhatsApp Web UI and a Tools add-on toggle); follow-up directives the same day (extract contacts; store as a separate list on the Local Plane; CSV export; build v2 in parallel)

## Screen-aware companion ask prototype (clicky parity)
- ID: TASK-027
- Status: done

Live 2026-07-29 validation on the user's machine with real grants and key found and fixed six defects — dotted Tauri event names (`annotate.marks`/`sensor.capture` had NEVER delivered), reasoning-model `<think>` leakage and empty answers, the missing `annotate*` capability entry that denied the annotation webview `plugin:event|listen`, an SVG `calc()` frame plus an opacity-0 entrance animation that prevented painting, and an ordinary always-on-top annotate window that could not draw over a fullscreen app — then proved summon, consented capture, correct screen description, rendered yellow spotlight+callout marks over a fullscreen app, ~12s auto-clear, and push-to-talk Whisper transcription. Full record and screenshot evidence: outputs/2026-07-29-task-027-companion-clicky-parity.md
