## K9 — Builder ladder: rung 3 steps, rung 4 structures
- ID: TASK-053
- Status: done (rung 3 DONE 2026-08-13, ADR-231/AP-151; rung 4 DONE 2026-09-02, ADR-271/AP-177 — the north-star test now passes over real wiring. Rung 5 is explicitly out of this task's Outcome and stays behind the K10 gate)
- Priority: P2
- Estimate: 5d
- Horizon: Core Modules
- Outcome: Rung 3 — the Capability Builder drafts automation STEPS from a promotion pattern + its ledger episodes, constrained to the existing skill registry + canonical step schema (constrained generation, not codegen; existing activation gates apply). Rung 4 — structure synthesis: the Builder proposes Databases/Views/Pages/blueprints from K3's entities and claims via the validated input-pack regeneration methodology (ADR-175/176/177; TASK-042 defines the per-class packs). Rung 5 (code-bearing skills via BA0 sandbox) is explicitly later, gated on rung 4 proving out.
- Prototype test: rung 3 — given a real promotion pattern, the Builder proposes steps that reference only registered skills and validate against the step schema, and an out-of-registry step is refused; rung 4 — the north-star test: given only observation data from ETA-style work, the Builder proposes a Deals/Sources/Theses-shaped module without being told about DealPilot. RUNG 3 PASSED — full path over real buildWiring (6 seeded ledger proposal+decision pairs → mined-shape signals → propose → accept → proposeSteps derives exactly the demonstrated step, validated by parseAutomationSteps, saved still-draft); out-of-registry skill refused with a structured reason, draft stays empty, empty draft cannot activate; live durable-boot walk 9/9 on real captured K7 focus data (rhythm pattern → structured pattern_not_skill_shaped refusal, stable across restart); real-browser drafts-card walk with the refusal rendered verbatim. Rung 4's north-star test remains unrun (blocked).
- Scope: `docs/raw/ai-harness-plan-2026-08-09.md` K9. Safe by construction: the View/Page schema cannot hold code (ADR-194).
- Evidence: ADR-175/176/177 regeneration methodology; TASK-042 pack definitions. Rung 3: ADR-231 — core `learning/builder.ts` (episodesForSkill payload-free envelope; draftStepsFromEpisodes modal-shape derivation, refusal-first), `learning.promotions.drafts.proposeSteps` lane, drafts-card "Draft steps from my decisions" control; core 6/6 + api 4/4, four mutations RED, verify 77/77. ~~HONEST BLOCKER for rung 4: TASK-042 has never been executed~~ — STALE as written; TASK-042 went done 2026-08-14 (ADR-238/AP-156) and this line was never updated. Rung 4 (2026-09-02, ADR-271): core `draftStructureFromClaims` — discriminator-or-field-signature grouping over K3 entities and claims, refusal-first, every proposed column carrying the support count and the live claim ids it was counted from; `learning.claims.proposeStructure` (a QUERY: rung 4 proposes a structure, it never creates one); a Second Brain "Show the shape of what Bridge has observed" control rendering the proposal or the refusal verbatim. **The north-star test PASSES**, twice: core 7/7 on an ETA fixture, and api 4/4 over real `buildWiring()` where all 27 claims are seeded through the governed propose→accept path first, so the Builder reads knowledge Bridge actually holds. Two mutations RED (red-tier filter disabled; STRUCTURE_MIN_ENTITIES 3→1). It is a DERIVATION, not a model call, and deliberately — a model could pass the north-star test by recognizing the domain rather than reading the data (ADR-271). The per-class input packs are NOT used: they govern generation, and rung 4 does not generate.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-09 ("start on AI harness"); user directive 2026-08-13 ("start on next tasks fr AI harness")
- Approval: AP-131 APPLIED; AP-151 APPLIED
- Dependencies: TASK-045 (rung 3, done); TASK-047 (done); TASK-042 (done 2026-08-14 — the "OPEN" here was stale)
- NOT DONE, carried out to TASK-090: neither rung attributes its work to an Agent Run. `CAPABILITY_BUILDER_AGENT` has a role, a scope and seeded governance in `wiring.ts`, but both Builder lanes execute as the requesting human — the Builder's own work leaves no attributable Run. Same in rung 3 as in rung 4, so fixing it here alone would make the ladder inconsistent.

## K11 — EG4 sensor tier: input, screen, ambient voice
- ID: TASK-054
- Status: in_progress
- Priority: P3
- Estimate: 8d
- Horizon: Hardening
- Outcome: Three senses on T0–T2 local tiers: (a) continuous input capture — clicks + AX-tree targets + typing as ACTUAL CHARACTER CONTENT (user decision 2026-08-14, AP-157, superseding the original event-only framing), behind the full fail-closed input boundary (OS secure-input, field-role gate where secure/password/undeterminable ⇒ characters suppressed — unknown = sensitive, app/domain denylist with private windows structurally excluded, never-persist-raw with on-device distillation, pattern redaction on the distilled layer); (b) continuous screen (xcap; post-meeting action drafts); (c) system audio → ambient voice (cidre/wasapi/libpulse; EG5). Raw processed on-device; only distilled Memory persists; raw never leaves the Local Plane.
- Prototype test: a password-field keystroke fixture yields at most "typed in an unknown field" (never characters); a denylisted app yields nothing including clicks; raw stream bytes are absent from disk after distillation (asserted over the capture directory); every distilled event is inspectable, deletable Memory with the Avatar blink fired.
- Scope: `docs/raw/ai-harness-plan-2026-08-09.md` K11 + "The input-capture boundary (K11a)". Rides ~80% on EG4's planned machinery (capture-core crates, permission model, AX-tree walker, capture→Memory+blink contract).
- Evidence: EG4/EG5 roadmap slices; harness-plan input-capture boundary. **K11a BOUNDARY SLICE DONE 2026-08-16 (ADR-239, under AP-157)** — the fail-closed distillation boundary, its API lane, and its consent surface exist and are provable; NOTHING captures yet (no event tap — see the honest blocker below). Shipped: `packages/core/src/learning/input-capture.ts` (one pure module owning the field-role gate with `undeterminable` failing closed structurally, the seed-floor denylist, the distiller, and the pattern-redaction backstop); the `input` capture source on unchanged K2 machinery (default OFF, kill switch, Human-only); `learning.capture.input.{policy,setDenylist,burst}` where `burst` RE-DISTILS server-side through the same core gate so a patched shell cannot widen what is stored, and the wire schema has no raw-keystroke field; `inputCaptureSignal` carrying a bucketed keyCount + disposition and never the text; a Settings card with a severity treatment and confirm-to-enable that the other five sources do not have. A REAL LEAK was found by the adversarial pass, not by review: the suppressed summary published the exact password LENGTH ("Typed 8 characters in a secure field") into a durable Memory row — now suppressed bursts carry no count at all, and two different-length secrets distil byte-identically. Verified: core 37/37 (contract suite + `input-capture.redteam.test.ts`, every safety test removal-fails), `@bridge/api` suite green over the real composition root, `pnpm verify` green.
- SECOND PASS 2026-08-16 (ADR-240): ran the lane against a real persistent composition root over an inherited-fd sidecar. Two findings. (a) FIXED — `burst` returned `captured: true` for a SUPPRESSED burst (a no-content marker had been written), so a caller branching on it would report a password field as captured; `captured` (content stored) and `recorded` (a row exists) are now separate, and a suppressed burst is `{ captured: false, recorded: true, verdict: "suppressed" }`. (b) OPEN, and a divergence from AP-157 — the lane stores NO typed text at all: `recordSignal()` has no body field, so the distilled redacted `content` is computed and dropped, and what persists is facets only (app, bundle, bucketed keyCount, disposition, time-of-day). That is functionally the event-only design the user rejected. Fail-safe, not leaky. Filed as BUGS.md evidence 2026-08-16; NOT fixed here because giving content a home means widening the `ObservedSignal`/`recordSignal` primitive that five metadata-only lanes share — a cross-lane privacy decision needing the user's call. Also added the lane's first API-level suite (`apps/api/test/input-capture-lane.test.ts`, 8/8 over the real root); before it, this lane had zero API coverage.
- THIRD PASS 2026-08-16 (ADR-241): harsh visual-critic pass on the RENDERED consent card found the copy claiming "Password managers and banking sites are always included" while `SEED_DENYLIST_DOMAINS` holds four password managers and ZERO banks (asserted in 4 code comments, 3 UI strings and ADR-239's summary). Fixed by making the claim true, not the list longer — seeding banks rejected as unmaintainable and falsely comforting. Also fixed: "Pause all" had disabled the OFF switch (an off-ramp is never disabled); the denylist rendered only AFTER consent though the dialog cited "apps you have not denied below"; the seed floor was editable text that silently reverted on save (now locked chips); the armed state was pixel-identical to the five benign sources (now an amber "Capturing" pill); the safety disclosure sat at ~2:1 contrast. Lesson: every boundary test passed while the sentence above the toggle was false — logic correctness and consent correctness are different properties.
- FOURTH PASS 2026-08-16 (ADR-242): second critic run verified the third pass's fixes and found THREE regressions the fixes themselves caused. (a) The new armed label "Capturing" keyed only on `enabled`, so it survived the kill switch — the row claimed "Capturing" in full amber while Pause all was on ("On" had been accidentally safe: it describes the consent switch, not live behaviour). (b) Pause hid the denylist entirely, though it is a policy editor, not a live control. (c) The "locked chips" had no chips — `--color-mist` is defined NOWHERE in the app, so it computed transparent and unremovable entries rendered as bare text with an orphan-wrapping padlock. Also fixed: a fetch failure silently returned `null`, deleting the exclusion list the consent dialog points at (now fails loud); WCAG 2.5.3 Label in Name; a stale test NAME still claiming banks; a stale comment calling the locked floor "removable-with-a-warning". Added a removal-fails test pinning the honest floor (`chase.com`/`hsbc.co.uk` NOT denied, `1password.com` denied). Verdict remains AAA: NO, but the remaining blockers are app-wide (the `window.confirm` consent pattern used in 7 places, heading/grouping semantics, the ~2:1 `--color-warm-gray` token), not K11-specific.
- K11b SLICE 2026-08-17 (ADR-243): the PRODUCER's pure core landed — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/providers/input.rs`, the burst accumulator plus the AX role/subrole → FieldRole mapping. Rust makes NO boundary decisions (core owns them) except one that only withholds MORE: a field not positively identified as ordinary text never accumulates characters or a count AT ALL, so a password never sits in the process heap awaiting discard and ADR-239's length property now holds BEFORE distillation. Secure is checked across role AND subrole before the ordinary-text allowlist (macOS reports a password box as role `AXTextField` + subrole `AXSecureTextField`; role-first would capture it). 12 tests; desktop lib 176/176; removal-fails PERFORMED — deleting the guard turned exactly 4 safety tests red, 8 non-safety green, then restored. STILL NOT BUILT: the CGEventTap and the AX focused-element read, so the lane remains inert.
- K11c SLICE 2026-08-17 (ADR-244): the TAP landed — `providers/input_tap.rs` (listen-only CGEventTap on key-down + per-keystroke AX focused-element role read) and the hub wiring (`sensor_list`/`sensor_start`/`sensor_stop`/`shutdown` + the `input_permission_status`/`input_request_permission` commands). Listen-only and returns every event untouched; macOS `IsSecureEventInputEnabled()` short-circuits to Secure BEFORE any AX call; a non-content field's key is never even decoded; the AX role is read fresh per key (not cached — a cache would let tabbing into a password box inherit the previous field's role) with a 25ms messaging timeout so an unresponsive app degrades to undeterminable rather than stalling the tap. Typed text goes to the RAW ring, never the Observation. FOUND DOING IT: `pump_channel` discarded the ring id, so a burst observation had no handle to its payload — now carried as `raw_id` (also fixes the same latent hole for clipboard). Two grants reported separately: no Input Monitoring ⇒ provider refuses to start; Input Monitoring without Accessibility ⇒ all fields unidentifiable ⇒ everything suppressed. 180/180, built in a clean worktree because a parallel session had lib.rs non-compiling. STILL NOT LIVE: no JS drain posts bursts to `learning.capture.input.burst` yet, and the end-to-end walk needs a signed build + both user grants.
- CONTENT-PERSISTENCE PASS 2026-08-17 (ADR-251, AP-159): the gap ADR-240 filed as an open user decision is CLOSED — AP-157's approved full-content capture is now actually delivered. `ObservedSignal` gains an optional `content?: string`, `recordSignal` persists it spread-if-present (`!== undefined`, so structural absence survives and an empty capture is not confused with no capture), and `inputCaptureSignal` maps the distilled redacted text onto the signal BODY, never `attributes` (those are the grouping facets the digest mines — prose there would make the user's words a pattern key). The user chose this from three options, over this session's recommendation of a dedicated input-only record kind; the rejected alternative is preserved in ADR-251. Mitigations inside the chosen option: the six metadata-only lanes' envelope types still admit no text field, plus a removal-fails guard asserting all six leave `content` undefined — PERFORMED (making the chat lane populate it turned the guard red naming the leaked string). Two things surfaced only because storage now exists: the card-number regex was consuming the space AFTER the match (`"pay with [redacted:card]today"`), missed by three tests that asserted only that the placeholder appeared — fixed with exact-output tests; and `!isLearningObservationEntry` in retrieval fusion / retrieval-eval / router memory search, written for relevance, is now the load-bearing guard keeping typed prose out of model prompts — pinned explicitly. The Settings copy "the only one that stores what you type" is TRUE as written for the first time, unedited. Core 728/728, API input lane 8/8 with the gap-documenting assertion inverted.
- K11d SLICE 2026-08-17 (ADR-252): the DRAIN landed — `apps/web/src/app/lib/input-capture-plan.ts` (the pure, import-free decision layer) + `input-capture-drain.ts` (the hook), mounted in `Layout.tsx` beside K7's. Follows K7's shape: 30s reconcile against `learning.capture.input.policy` driving `sensor_start`/`sensor_stop`, 5s drain, no retry queue. Consent stops the TAP, not just the API's acceptance of its output. The text is not in the Observation (ADR-014 — it sits in the local raw ring behind `sensor_read_raw(raw_id)`), so the drain fetches it deliberately AND re-checks the field role first, making this the third of three independent refusals; `raw_id != null` was rejected as the condition because it trusts the producer. The planner returns a shape with NO `text` field, so it cannot emit content in principle. 8 tests over the real source module via the `ts-resolve` loader; removal-fails PERFORMED (bare `raw_id` check → exactly the two suppressed-role tests red, other six green).
- K11b/K11c BOUNDARY SLICE 2026-08-17 (ADR-253): the remaining two senses got their fail-closed boundaries, built before any producer per the K10 gate. `screen-capture.ts` keys on the WINDOW not the screen (a frame has no natural sensitive unit), honours macOS `NSWindowSharingNone` as the `IsSecureEventInputEnabled` analogue, suppresses private/incognito windows EVEN when shareable (K8 already refuses incognito; a screen sensor must not be the back door), and strips the window TITLE from suppressed frames (ADR-239's leak class). `audio-capture.ts` confronts the one thing no gate can fix — **the speaker may not be the consenter** — by making it structural: ambient audio needs an explicit per-session ARM (checked before participants are characterised), and a multi-party session stores nothing without an ATTRIBUTABLE `allPartyConsentAssertedBy` user id (a boolean is satisfiable by a stale default); loopback is inherently multi-party and cannot reach the solo path. **The CONTENT decision is left OPEN for both** — derived text and transcripts default OFF behind opt-in allowlists, because "post-meeting action drafts" is the same content-vs-metadata call AP-157 settled for keystrokes and the plan records NO decision for either; a content mode is not a gate bypass. Three separate denylists (one list would let the narrowest consent widen the others); the audio floor is telephony/personal calls, not password managers. Found doing it: `defaultAudioCaptureDenylist()` copied seeds raw while matching lowercases the probe, so capitalised seeds (FaceTime) denied NOTHING on the default path — screen had it latently, both now route through one merge, K11a checked and unaffected. 30 tests; core 755/755; removal-fails PERFORMED across six mutations.
- HONEST BLOCKER: **K11a is connected end to end in code and still NOT PROVEN LIVE; K11b and K11c are INERT.** For K11a the live walk needs a signed build plus both TCC grants and real typing (build done + signed 2026-08-17, DR certificate-anchored; grants and typing are the user's step). For K11b/K11c nothing exists above the boundary — no consent source, no producer, no API lane, no Settings surface — and the content question for both awaits the user's decision. Also unbuilt against this task's OWN declared outcome: CLICKS and AX-tree targets (the tap masks key-down only), and the Input Monitoring permission UI (`input_permission_status`/`input_request_permission` are registered but nothing calls them). K11(b) continuous screen and K11(c) system audio remain unstarted. The live walk that proves capture end-to-end additionally needs the user to grant Input Monitoring AND Accessibility to a signed build and type real keys — the same shape of user step K7's Accessibility grant required; the user agreed on 2026-08-17 to perform it once the drain path lands.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-09 ("start on AI harness")
- Approval: AP-131 APPLIED (queueing); AP-157 APPLIED 2026-08-14 (keystroke content-vs-events decision RESOLVED — full content capture behind the fail-closed boundary; build authorized); AP-159 APPLIED 2026-08-17 (content-persistence gap resolved by widening the shared `ObservedSignal` with an optional `content` field — AP-157 DELIVERED, not reversed).
- Dependencies: TASK-043 (K10 gate, done), TASK-051 (done). The keystroke content-vs-events decision is resolved (AP-157) — no open user gate remains before build.

## Companion trigger and capture parity — Fn hold, double-click listen, on-request recording
- ID: TASK-055
- Status: in_progress (2026-09-04; the Fn-hold summon shipped under TASK-099 as a sustained-hold poll of the Function flag, no Input Monitoring needed; double-click listen and on-request recording remain open)
- Priority: P3
- Estimate: 3d
- Horizon: Convergence
- Outcome: The companion ask panel can be summoned by holding Fn (in addition to the existing ⌘⇧Space) and by double-clicking the Avatar to toggle listening mode; a user-requested screen recording capability exists with an explicit start/stop control, defined local storage location, and retention policy — all three preserving the existing consent, taint, and no-silent-sensing invariants from TASK-027.
- Prototype test: (1) holding Fn from another app summons the ask panel and begins capturing audio for at least as long as Fn is held, released Fn stops capture, exactly as ⌘⇧Space does today; (2) double-clicking the Avatar toggles listening mode on, a second interaction (per the agreed stop condition) toggles it off, with no change to the existing hover-click ✨ or right-click menu paths; (3) an explicit "record my screen" request starts a visible, revocable recording with a clear on-screen indicator and an explicit stop control, the file lands under the Local Plane at a named path, is associated with the requesting Module/Record per the standard Files rules, and is governed by a stated retention/deletion policy — no recording starts without an explicit per-request consent tick, matching the existing screenshot consent copy pattern.
- Scope: docs/raw/desktop-companion-agent-roadmap-2026-07.md (companion trigger surface); outputs/2026-07-29-task-027-companion-clicky-parity.md (existing consent/taint baseline to preserve). NEW for this task: (a) Fn-hold trigger — macOS does not expose Fn through `tauri-plugin-global-shortcut`'s normal `Code`/`Modifiers` API, so this needs a low-level modifier-flags event tap (`NSEvent`/`CGEventTap`-equivalent) registered Rust-side, same mechanism Invoko itself relies on, not a shortcut-string swap; (b) double-click-to-listen — an `onDoubleClick` handler on the Avatar wired to the same start-listening path `CompanionAsk.tsx`/`companion_ask_start` uses today, with an explicit toggle-off condition to design (second click vs. silence timeout) since double-click has no natural "hold" analogue; (c) on-request screen recording — NEW capture surface, not a variant of the existing single-shot `capture_display_jpeg`; before implementation, an explicit design/approval decision is needed on: storage location and format, retention/auto-deletion window, the visible recording indicator, the stop control, whether recordings are ever eligible for cloud egress (default should be no, matching the screenshot consent model), and Files/Module association per the UI architecture rules. RESOLVED by AP-118: 24-hour rolling local retention with automatic deletion, Local-Plane-only with NO cloud egress ever (stricter than the screenshot consent model), an Avatar red-ring + menu-bar visible indicator, an explicit stop command, and a hard automatic safety time cap. (a), (b), and (c) are all unblocked; none are implemented yet.
- Evidence: none yet (task creation only)
- Requests: R-043; R-044 (screen-recording design questions)
- Approval: AP-117 applied for task creation and queue position; AP-118 applied for the screen-recording design decision (retention/egress/indicator/stop-control)
- Dependencies: TASK-027 (done) — reuses its consent/taint/blink-tell invariants and existing ask-panel/annotate surfaces; must not regress its V-verification results

## Multi-app automation for the background agent
- ID: TASK-056
- Status: ready
- Priority: P2
- Estimate: 8d
- Horizon: Core Modules
- Outcome: TASK-028's background research agent generalizes from browser-only to arbitrary installed macOS apps — planning steps, reading real app content via a governed Accessibility-API actuator, and acting only behind an approved Proposal — with every step an inspectable child Run and every read of external app content taint-tagged and quarantined from the planner's instruction channel exactly as TASK-028 already does for web pages.
- Prototype test: from a real Organization, start a Run for an objective spanning at least two non-browser apps; it reads app content via Accessibility APIs (not screenshots-only), refuses to click/type without an approved Proposal, records per-action citations plus `untrusted_external` taint on content read from other apps, survives an app restart, and reports rather than obeys instructions embedded in app content aimed at the agent.
- Scope: docs/raw/autonomous-browser-research-agent-plan-2026-07.md (BR0/BR3/BR4/BR5 pattern to generalize); docs/raw/desktop-companion-agent-roadmap-2026-07.md. NEW: an Accessibility-API actuator layer beyond `research_webview.rs`'s contained browser surface; a per-app allowlist and permission model (which installed apps the actuator may read/act in, and under what grant) — this is a named separate design decision, not assumed, mirroring how TASK-028 itself sequenced AP-088 (click/type authority) ahead of AP-089 (engine choice).
- Evidence: none yet (task creation only)

## UI standardization convergence — one toolbar, one shell, one dropdown
- ID: TASK-061
- Status: done (2026-09-03, LANDED — the live desktop pass is TASK-041's, see below)
- Priority: P1
- Estimate: 3d
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: every page in `platform/apps/web` renders its data through ONE toolbar in §5's canonical slot order (List → View → Search → Filter → Custom actions → 3-dots → Insights chevron), so a user moving between Modules sees the same controls in the same places. Today two toolbars coexist — `StandardToolbar` (§5-conformant, ONE consumer: ApprovalsPage) and `<DataViews>`'s own inline row (the one the Module pages actually render, with no List dropdown, no Insights chevron, and Add-column outside the 3-dots) — which is the mechanical reason "every page still looks different".
- Prototype test: `KNOWN_DIVERGENCES` in `platform/apps/web/test/ui-conformance.test.mjs` is empty and the array is deleted; a live desktop pass over DealPilot, JobPilot, Relationship, Task Manager, Signals, Approvals and Artifacts shows identical toolbar slot order and identical dropdown behaviour (selected-first, search, pinned Add) on every one.
- Scope: converge `<DataViews>`'s inline toolbar into `StandardToolbar`; route the 10 ratchet entries through `ModuleSurfaceLayout`+`DataViews` (ArtifactsPage, ApprovalsPage, ResearchRunsPage, RelationshipSubmodulePage, OrganizationPage, SecondBrainPage) or exempt them with a reason; move Sort and Add column into the 3-dots; add the fixed New Element row above the column summary; wire cell right-click to `StandardRowMenu` and column right-click to `StandardColumnMenu` (§5f); Form view — drop any Build/Preview split, add click-outside autosave and the share affordance under §5's list/view permission model.
- Evidence: adoption audit + root-cause post-mortem recorded in `docs/raw/ui-architecture-rules-2026-07.md` §10 (2026-08-10). Landed this session: the conformance gate itself, `StandardDropdown` (§5e) with `ListDropdown` and the View switcher converted onto it, and red-flag click-to-remove (§5).
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-10 ("Can you also audit all pages if they use the standardized view entity because every page still looks different to me"; "Critically evaluate why all these rules I had shared earlier got lost and ensure this behaviour doesnt repeat with corrective measures")
- Approval: user directive 2026-08-10 recorded as APPLIED for task creation, queue position, and the §0/§5e/§5f/§5g/§10 canon additions
- Dependencies: none
- LANDED 2026-09-03 (ADR-278): **there is one toolbar.** `StandardToolbar.tsx` and its `ListDropdown` wrapper are DELETED — Approvals, their only consumer, now renders its review queue through `<ModuleSurfaceLayout>` + `<DataViews>` like every Module Page, so the row users see and the row §5 describes are the same object. `KNOWN_DIVERGENCES` is empty, the array and its stale-entry test are deleted, and a new gate fails the build if a second toolbar component reappears or the §5 slot order in `<DataViews>` is reordered.
- THE SEVEN RATCHET ENTRIES, each closed honestly: **ResearchRunsPage** and **ApprovalsPage** converted to the shell (both are Databases — Runs and pending Proposals — and both lost a bespoke sidebar list that was the reason they looked unlike everything else); **SecondBrainPage** moved onto `<ModuleSurfaceLayout>`; **OrganizationPage** and **RelationshipSubmodulePage** EXEMPT with written reasons (one stacks several Databases down an auto-height page, which the layout's single table region cannot express; the other owns no rows at all); **RelationshipHelpdeskPage** and **SettingsPage** were already exempt from the shell, and the primitive scan now honours EXEMPT — a page excused from the shell with a reason cannot then be required to use the shell's slots.
- Also landed: Research Runs are started from the standard New gesture (the Record page from TASK-083) rather than a page-local form, so the objective is typed on the same surface every other Record is created on. FormView's **Build/Preview split is deleted** (Build only listed fields the spec already renders) and click-outside autosave lands for an EXISTING Record only — a new one still writes nothing until Save (C-34/ADR-259), because autosaving a half-typed new Record is the defect that rule exists to prevent.
- NOT LANDED: the live desktop pass over the seven surfaces. Every check here is static or headless; "identical toolbar slot order and identical dropdown behaviour on every one" needs the user's machine (TASK-041). The §5f column right-click was already live and is unchanged.
- Evidence 2026-09-03: web suite 220/220 with the rewritten gate (shell backlog 0, primitive backlog 0), `check:ui-rules` and `check:vocabulary` clean, web typecheck clean. Two `sub-12px-type` occurrences left the baseline with the deleted files and are recorded.

## Persist ViewConfig — the unlock behind saved views, lists, sharing and linked views
- ID: TASK-062
- Status: in_progress (built, wired and tested 2026-09-03; only the browser leg of the exit test is outstanding — TASK-041)
- Priority: P1
- Estimate: 3d
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: a named View survives reload. `ViewConfig` is React state today, so every filter, sort, column-visibility choice and List selection is discarded on refresh — which is why Lists, view sharing, personal-vs-collaborative views and linked views are all absent despite the UI for several of them existing. One `view_config` table (id, database_id, name, owner, scope, kind, filters, sorts, groups, hidden_columns, widths) plus CRUD delivers five benchmark capabilities from one schema change.
- Prototype test: create a filtered+sorted View on DealPilot Deals, name it, reload the browser, and it returns exactly as configured; the same View appears in the List dropdown for a second session; a second Database shows its own Views and never the first's.
- Scope: T1/P1 of `docs/raw/capability-audit-notion-airtable-evernote-2026-08-10.md`. Prerequisite for TASK-064 (sharing) and for the personal-vs-collaborative ownership model in ui-architecture-rules §5.
- Evidence: capability audit 2026-08-10 §3 — measured from `platform/packages/tables/src/types.ts` and `dataviews/DataViews.tsx`; no persistence path existed in the repo at task creation.
- LANDED 2026-09-03: one `view_configs` table (migration 0047) + `ViewConfigStore` in core (in-memory double) + `DrizzleViewConfigStore` + `view.saved.list/save/update/remove` + `useSavedViews` wired into `<DataViews>` itself. The shell owns it deliberately: fifteen pages render through `<DataViews>`, and a per-page opt-in would be fifteen chances to forget — every one of them gained saved Lists in this change without being edited. A saved View restores BOTH halves of what was on screen: the `ViewConfig` and the column visibility the shell keeps outside it. The §5 List slot's "not built yet (TASK-062)" disabled reason is deleted and its Add row is live; a `ui-conformance` test fails if that placeholder reason ever comes back.
- OWNERSHIP, and why the second scope exists from the first migration: a View is `personal` (owner-only) or `organization` (readable by every member, writable ONLY by its owner). Read and write are different predicates in the RLS policy on purpose — a shared View anyone could rewrite is not a share — and that asymmetry is exactly the seam TASK-064 extends rather than a scope invented for symmetry. `personal` is the default: a View is never shared by omission.
- The `ViewConfig` shape is validated at the ROUTER edge (`savedViewConfigSchema`, `.strict()`), not in the table: the shape belongs to the view kinds, and a column that encoded it would migrate every time a kind gained a field. `.strict()` is load-bearing — an unknown key would make the stored config a place to carry state past every validator downstream.
- Verified 2026-09-03: `@bridge/core` view-config 5/5 (personal invisible to others; organization readable but NOT writable, reported as not-found rather than forbidden; no crossing of Databases or Organizations; one name per Database per owner on create AND rename, case- and space-insensitive; update stamps `updatedAt` without rewriting `createdAt`); api `saved-views` 5/5 over the real `buildWiring()` (round-trip after a reload-shaped read, per-Database isolation, an unknown config key REFUSED rather than stored, name collision as CONFLICT, overwrite-in-place and delete); web suite green; api, web, core and db typechecks clean.
- WHICH List a browser had open is per-viewer localStorage, not server state — losing it costs one click, and putting it on the server would make one machine's UI state everyone else's. That is what makes the exit test's "reload and it returns exactly as configured" a reload rather than a re-selection.
- NOT DONE: no delete affordance in the dropdown (the procedure exists and is tested; `StandardDropdown` has no per-row action slot, and adding one is TASK-061's convergence work, not a bespoke menu here); no UI for the `organization` scope yet, so every View saved from the app today is personal — the scope is reachable through `view.saved.update` and is where TASK-064 starts; and the browser leg of the exit test (create, reload, see it return) needs the user's machine like every other live check (TASK-041).
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-10 (capability audit vs Notion/Airtable/Evernote)
- Approval: user directive 2026-08-10 recorded as APPLIED for task creation and queue position
- Dependencies: none

## Record metadata columns from the Event log
- ID: TASK-063
- Status: done (2026-09-03, LANDED on Task Manager — a second surface is one spec edit, see below)
- Priority: P2
- Estimate: 2d
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: `created_time`, `created_by`, `last_edited_time`, `last_edited_by` exist as real ColumnKinds derived from the Event log rather than stored twice. Both Notion and Airtable treat these as built-ins, and activity feeds, staleness Automations and "recently edited" Views all depend on them.
- Prototype test: a Record written through the governed pipeline shows a correct created/last-edited actor and timestamp in a table View; sorting by last-edited orders rows correctly; the values are derived, not writable by a Form or inline edit.
- Scope: T1/P2 of the capability audit. Cheapest high-leverage field gap — the data already exists in Events.
- Evidence: capability audit 2026-08-10 §1 — Bridge has 11 of ~26 benchmark field types; these four are the lowest-cost of the missing set.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-10
- Approval: user directive 2026-08-10 recorded as APPLIED for task creation and queue position
- Dependencies: none
- LANDED 2026-09-03 (ADR-280): `createdTime`, `createdBy`, `lastEditedTime`, `lastEditedBy` are real `ColumnKind`s in `@bridge/tables` with `isMetadataColumn` as the one predicate every surface asks. `records.metadata` folds them from the `events` table — the fold lives in `apps/api/src/record-metadata.ts` so it is testable without a database — and `<DataViews>` merges them into the rows BEFORE search and sort, which is what makes "sort by last edited" order on the real value rather than on empty cells. Derived means unwritable, structurally: `isFormEditable` refuses them, the Record page lists them with what fills them, and Change-type is handed no kind at all because there is no type they could become.
- THE ACTOR IS READ FROM DECLARED KEYS, NOT SNIFFED: `events.payload` is free-form jsonb, so the five keys Bridge's own writers use are listed in one reviewable constant and anything else reads `null` — an honest empty cell rather than a plausible id (ADR-247). A Record with no Events reads unknown in every field rather than borrowing its row's arrival time.
- WIRED on Task Manager (`recordEntityType="task"`), the surface whose Events carry `entityType: "task"`. Any other Database joins by adding the four columns and its own `recordEntityType` — one spec edit per surface and no per-page fetch code, because the shell owns the read.
- NOT LANDED: the in-memory mode keeps no durable Event log, so `EmptyRecordMetadataSource` returns nothing and the columns render blank there — deliberate, since inventing a creation time in a mode with no ledger is the fabrication ADR-247 forbids. No browser pass (TASK-041).
- Evidence 2026-09-03: api `record-metadata` 5/5 (earliest/latest fold whatever the arrival order, single-Event case, entity isolation, unknown-actor, empty), web 241/241 with a new conformance gate that fails if any surface makes a metadata column writable or the shell stops filling them, `procedure-classification` 5/5, tables 21/21.

## Scoped share grants — wire the built Share panel to Views and Forms
- ID: TASK-064
- Status: in_progress (2026-09-03 — the grant primitive is LANDED and the Share panel acts; the data-plane half is named below, not claimed)
- Priority: P1
- Estimate: 5d
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: the Share panel recovered from `claude/bridge-ai-launch-e29e00` stops being honestly-disabled and actually shares. Bridge has exactly one sharing primitive today (`helpdeskTickets.accessToken`) and it is not connected to Views; generalize it into a scoped share grant (target, scope, access level, expiry, revocation) so a View or Form can be shared at view-only, edit, or co-owner level — which is what ui-architecture-rules §5 already declares as canon with no implementation behind it.
- Prototype test: share a filtered View at view-access with a second identity; that identity can edit visible cells, cannot read a hidden column or a filtered-out row, and gains both only when promoted to co-owner; revoking the grant closes access immediately; a published Form accepts a submission through the ordinary governed insert path.
- Scope: T1/P3 of the capability audit. Depends on TASK-062 because a share grant needs a persisted View to point at.
- Evidence: capability audit 2026-08-10 §3/§4; the Share UI exists and is disabled with its reason (commit eed00466, AP-021).
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-10 (attached Share panel screenshot)
- Approval: user directive 2026-08-10 recorded as APPLIED for task creation and queue position
- Dependencies: TASK-062 (ready)
- LANDED 2026-09-03 (ADR-279): `share_grants` (migration 0048) generalizes `helpdeskTickets.accessToken` into a scoped grant — target, access level (`view` ⊂ `edit` ⊂ `coowner`), optional expiry, revocation — over a saved View. `view.share.list/grant/revoke/resolve` are human-only and owner-only (co-owner may re-share); the link token is minted server-side, never accepted from the client. **Revocation is a write, not a delete**: the grant stays on the record and reports itself dead, because "who could see this last week" is the question a share ledger exists to answer. `usable` is computed by the kernel's rule and returned, so no surface does its own expiry arithmetic. The Share panel in the List dropdown issues and revokes real grants and shows the token — it does NOT print a share URL, because no route opens a shared View yet and a plausible-looking link would be a fabricated capability (ADR-247).
- THE RLS CHANGE THAT MAKES A SHARE REAL: migration 0047's `view_configs_read` admitted owner + `organization` scope only, so a grant on a personal View would have granted nothing. 0048 replaces it to also admit a row a LIVE grant names the caller on — expiry and revocation inside the predicate, so access ends at the database rather than at whichever caller remembers to check. `InMemoryViewConfigStore` takes the grant store for the same reason: a double that answered differently from production would be worse than none.
- NOT LANDED — stated plainly rather than implied: the prototype test's DATA half. "cannot read a hidden column or a filtered-out row" is enforced today on the VIEW (a recipient's `resolve` carries the sharer's hidden-column list and the server's `canEdit`/`canReshare`), not on the Module data a View points at: Bridge's Module read procedures take an identity, not a grant, so a recipient's rows are still their own RLS-scoped rows. Closing that means every Module read accepting a grant and applying the View's filters server-side — a cross-surface Tier C change with its own row. There is also no route that OPENS a shared View, so the second-identity walkthrough cannot be performed end to end, and a published Form still has no submission path of its own.
- Evidence 2026-09-03: core `share-grant` 5/5 (ladder totality, revoked/expired decided in one place, strongest-live-grant, link token reachable only by token, idempotent revoke), api `share-grants` 5/5 over the real wiring, `procedure-classification` + `public-cloud-boundary` 7/7 with the four new procedures classified, migration-metadata gate rebased through 0048, web conformance 19/19 including the new Share gate.
- Full-suite state 2026-09-03 for all three rows landed this session: core 799/799, db 239/239, web 240/240, api green except two load-induced failures in files none of this work touched (`chat-model-manager`, `jobpilot-culture-research`) — both 63/63 when re-run on their own, having failed only while three suites shared the machine and one of them asserts a 120s socket-close deadline.

- Approval: user directive 2026-08-10 recorded as APPLIED for task creation, queue position, and the §3b canon addition

What this row still owns is everything that makes Notes a Record BODY and Governance a provenance surface: rich text, checklists, templates, revision history with restore, save-by-email routing, inline attachments; and per-field provenance, the Proposal/approval trail, open red flags with their learning state, per-field residency and the read/edit/comment/co-own model. The plain textarea shipped by TASK-083 is a floor, not this outcome — do not read the existing Section as the row being done.

## Desktop shell survives Local Plane loss instead of aborting
- ID: TASK-066
- Status: done
- Priority: P0
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: A sidecar stall, crash, or kill is survivable. The shell never aborts on native teardown, a transient stall is not mistaken for Local Plane loss, and a genuinely dead sidecar is restarted in place on the same port and token rather than ending the session.
- Prototype test: With the app running, `kill -9` the sidecar node PID. The log must show `restarting it (attempt 1/3)` then `recovered on http://127.0.0.1:<same port>`, `grep -c "fatal runtime error"` must stay 0, and the shell process must still be alive. Repeating past the 3-per-10-minute budget must fail closed with a Local Plane loss verdict rather than looping.
- Scope: ADR-229. Closes the `__rust_foreign_exception` abort class that BUG-2026-07-30 declared "closed by construction" on 2026-07-31 and that recurred with a different trigger on 2026-08-06. Four changes: time-based liveness (6 s continuous unreachability, 3 s probe) replacing a failed-probe count; `demote_panel_before_teardown` generalizing the guard `overlay.rs` already had to every retired window; `guard_native_teardown` wrapping native teardown in `objc2::exception::catch`; and `api_sidecar::restart` respawning the child on the retained loopback listener with the original token. Two adjacent defects fixed under the same evidence: debug builds preferring a stale staged sidecar over the fresh monorepo build, and the notch geometry read losing its cutout at startup so the companion parked away from the notch.
- Evidence: `docs/BUGS.md` 2026-08-06 entry, RESOLVED 2026-08-12 with the captured `NSRangeException` / `AnnotatePanel` exception — the datum the entry recorded as never obtained. Live verification: three launches with zero aborts where the pre-fix binary aborted deterministically ~35 s in; a forced `kill -9` recovering on the same port with a new child PID; a real transient caught in an ordinary session (`tolerating for up to 6s` → `reachable again`).
- Requests: user reports 2026-08-12, verbatim: *"Currently the desktop app is not working properly and crashing a lot. Can you check and fix all the perf issues? It is eating up lot RAM also"*, *"it says local plane unavailable"* (repeated), *"the notch part is coming elsewhere"*
- Approval: none needed (defect repair; ADR-229 records the design decisions)
- Dependencies: none
- Invariants this task establishes — breaking either reopens the abort class: every window promoted with `to_panel` MUST be demoted with `panel.to_window()` before `destroy()`, and every native teardown path MUST stay inside `guard_native_teardown`.

## Academics Module — Subjects/Lecture Sessions/Assignments vault
- ID: TASK-069
- Status: done
- Priority: P2
- Estimate: 3d
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: a new `academics` Module (P1 of the plan) with three sibling toggles — Subjects, Lecture Sessions, Assignments — database-backed, using the shared `records`/`TableSpec` mechanism, no bespoke per-module data store. Local Files land under `~/Documents/Bridge/<Organization>/Academics/`. **Scope change 2026-09-04** (ADR 2026-09-04 "The Egg ships the kernel; Modules live in Commons"): the Module is a Commons manifest with three declared Databases (`module.databases[]`) and no code of its own — the standard Module Page (UI Rulebook §3d) renders each Page and `moduleRecords.*` serves the rows from the Local Plane. The Egg does not seed it (`EGG_MODULES` unchanged). The original "Subject's Record Detail shows related Sections" line is dropped: the standard Page has no Record Detail Sections and no relation picker yet.
- Prototype test: boot the Egg profile, register the Academics manifest, approve the install proposal, insert a Subject, a Lecture Session and an Assignment through `moduleRecords.insert`, list each Page — real rows, never dummy; the Module row passes `packages/module-manifests/test/catalog.test.ts`.
- Scope (as landed): `platform/packages/module-manifests/src/index.ts` — `academicsDatabases` (Subjects: name/code/term/instructor/credits; Lecture Sessions: subject `relation`→`academics.subjects`, date, topic, notes_summary, recording_link `url`; Assignments: subject relation, title, due_date, status select `not_started/in_progress/submitted/graded`, grade), Pages point at the declared Database ids, version 0.2.0, Commons entry with `need:coursework-vault`, `BUILT_IN_SOURCE_REFS` points at the manifest. Deleted the bespoke surface (`apps/web/src/app/pages/AcademicsPage.tsx`, `apps/api/src/routers/academics.ts`, `packages/db/src/academics-store.ts`, their router/wiring/deployment-boundary/route entries) so `/module/academics/:page` falls through to `ModulePage`. Study Steward Agent (`plane: local`) declared with no Skills wired yet — Skills (lecture-synthesis, syllabus-intake, recall-scheduler, reference-resolve, workload-forecast) and their Automations are follow-on tasks, not this one. Files: `module-files.ts` derives the folder from the Module display name ("Academics") — no extra code.
- NOT LANDED: (1) the drizzle tables `academics_subjects`/`academics_lecture_sessions`/`academics_assignments` (migration 0042) stay in the schema — dropping them is a schema change that could destroy rows an install wrote through the old router; removal condition: a migration that copies any rows into `module:records:academics.*` first. (2) The `relation` columns are stored as declared but the standard Page has no relation picker (ADR 2026-09-04 consequences), so the Subject is typed by hand until the picker lands. (3) Records written through the old `academics.*` router are not migrated into the Local Plane namespace.
- Evidence: `apps/api/test/academics-module.test.ts` and the catalog test seen failing before the manifest declared Databases ("Module academics declares no Database subjects in its manifest"; catalog `databases` deepEqual), green after; `node --test dist/test/module-records.test.js dist/test/egg-boot.test.js dist/test/egg-profile.test.js dist/test/modules.test.js dist/test/academics-module.test.js` 45/45; module-manifests 26/26; api + web typecheck clean; `check-retired-vocabulary` and `check-ui-rules` OK.

## Events sub-module (NetworkManager) — speaker extraction + LinkedIn outreach queue
- ID: TASK-070

- Outcome: an `events` sub-module (`parentModule: "relationship"`) under NetworkManager tracking conference/event links, plus a manual speaker-extraction Automation that fetches an Event URL, extracts speaker rows, resolves identity through OpenAlex/ORCID/Crossref under the existing 3-tier match gate (strong/moderate/flag), and drafts People + Event→Person edges for approval — reusing the WhatsApp Contact Extractor's draft-then-approve staging. A second, human-in-the-loop outreach queue drafts personalized LinkedIn connection notes from resolved speakers for the owner to send manually — there is no automated LinkedIn send capability (no LinkedIn API exposes invitations; their User Agreement prohibits automated access; `Tools/recon-salvage-2026-08-03/README.md:192` already codifies "LinkedIn is never fetched directly").

- Scope: manifest entry (own capability, since `parentModule` grants nothing per ADR-178 — must declare `readPrivate("person")`/`writePrivate("person")` itself), one Page/TableSpec, route, page component, catalog test update. Extraction pipeline (fetch/extract → OpenAlex resolve → 3-tier gate → draft write) and the outreach-queue drafting Skill are follow-on implementation work under this Task, sequenced after the manifest/page skeleton lands.
- Evidence: none — greenfield. Confirmed via repo-wide grep (incl. `git log --all --grep`) that no "dice"/"conference"/"speaker" process exists in this repo; "dice" only ever means the Dice string-similarity coefficient (`platform/packages/dedupe/src/scoring.ts:1`); "conference" only appears as an unbuilt future Tool (`platform/tools/recorder/package.json:6`, `docs/raw/calendar-plan.md:270`).
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-13, verbatim: *"add a sub-module to Relationships module called Events where I can add conference land events links. Similar to dice conference process, I need a workflow automation to extract speaker names and details, an automation to send linkedin connection request."* Corrected in the same turn: no matching prior process exists in-repo, and unattended LinkedIn sending is not built — see Outcome.

## Accounting and D2C land as Modules, on the Local Plane, with donor history preserved
- ID: TASK-074
- Status: done (2026-08-16, live-verified)
- Priority: P1
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: Avilo runs inside Bridge as the **Accounting** Module and CV Naturals as the **D2C** Module (with Orders/Inventory as D2C's own toggle Pages and Research/Notes as `parent: d2c` sub-modules), both on the Local Plane over better-sqlite3, both declaring a manifest, both reachable from Bridge's nav with manifest-driven detail. `@bridge/whatsapp` serves D2C; CVN's clone is not imported. Neither donor repository is modified.
- Prototype test: from a clean Bridge checkout, `pnpm verify` is green with both Modules in the workspace; the Bridge desktop shell lists Accounting and D2C in nav, D2C expands to its four sub-modules, and opening Accounting renders a real imported client period from a sqlite database — not an empty state and not a fixture. `git log --follow` on an imported Avilo source file reaches that file's pre-merge commits. `git -C <each donor> status` is clean and its HEAD unmoved.
- Scope: Stage 2 of [`docs/raw/module-merge-accounting-d2c-2026-08-16.md`](raw/module-merge-accounting-d2c-2026-08-16.md) §9. Subtree merges with `--allow-unrelated-histories`; module trees under `platform/modules/{accounting,d2c,d2c-orders,d2c-inventory,d2c-research,d2c-production}`; a manifest entry per Module; whatsapp dedupe; the §8 id-renumbering map produced and landed as §8a of that doc.
- Evidence: three-way id collision measured 2026-08-16 (Bridge ADR 006–229 / TASK 001–066 / AP 001–147; Avilo ADR 001–053 / BUG 001–047; CVN ADR 001–016 / TASK 001–040). `diff -rq` on the two whatsapp `src/` trees returned no differences — all 16 files byte-identical.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-16, verbatim: *"Merge Avilo and CV Naturals as modules within relationship OS called Accounting and D2C respectively"*; *"Keep the original avilo and cvn untouched. Only optimize bridge"*
- Approval: AP-162 APPLIED (ADR-246)
- Dependencies: none
- Both original blockers RESOLVED 2026-08-16. CVN's `module-host` is an orphaned `dist/` with no `package.json` and no importers — dead weight, not a divergent copy, not imported. And the Local Plane turned out to be **PGlite, not sqlite** (Bridge has no sqlite anywhere), which needs no kernel change: a Module opens its own database inside the host-granted `ModuleContext.dataDir`, exactly what that contract was designed for. Two embedded engines coexist and never meet.
- Landed: `platform/modules/accounting` (35 donor commits), `platform/modules/d2c` (3), `platform/packages/module-host` (2); `@bridge/whatsapp` serves D2C and the byte-identical CVN clone was not imported; six manifests (Accounting, D2C, and D2C's Research/Notes sub-modules, with Orders/Inventory as D2C's toggle Pages per AP-011). Accounting's 168 tests and D2C's 34 pass. `pnpm verify` 90/92, the 2 failures pre-existing on `main` and filed in BUGS.md 2026-08-16.
- Landed 2026-08-16 (part 2): `accounting-store.ts`/`d2c-store.ts` open real `better-sqlite3` files inside `ctx.wiring` (`accountingDb`/`d2cDb`, never `@bridge/db`/PGlite); `router.ts` gained `accounting`/`d2c`/`d2cResearch`/`d2cNotes` tRPC routers backed by those connections (Clients/Reports, Orders/Inventory, Plant Records, Notes — real queries against the imported Drizzle schemas, no fabricated rows); `AccountingPage.tsx`, `D2CPage.tsx` (Orders+Inventory), `D2CResearchPage.tsx`, `D2CNotesPage.tsx` follow the `ModuleSurfaceLayout`/`DataViews` shell with honest empty states and route in `app/routes.tsx` via `requireBuiltInModule`. Live-verified in a running dev server (`BRIDGE_LOCAL_DIR` local-plane boot): all six Pages render with real `0`-row empty states, an honest `insertDisabledReason`, and the seeded Governance Section — no console errors, no fixture data. `pnpm verify`'s build/typecheck/test:coverage steps are green across all 62 workspace packages except the two pre-existing failures already filed in BUGS.md (2026-08-16); `check:vocabulary` also fails only on the same pre-existing empty-baseline bug (BUGS.md), not on anything new.
- P&L formulas, PDF import, WhatsApp order capture, GST invoicing and the stock-costing engine stay out of scope for this task — the Prototype test asked for a real, running, sqlite-backed Module surface, not feature parity with either donor app.

## Every Module declares what it is allowed to do, and the engine enforces it
- ID: TASK-072
- Status: done (2026-08-16, live-verified)
- Priority: P1
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: every Module carries a Governance Section directly below its Intelligence Section, showing the `allow`/`deny` policy declared in its manifest, editable by the user at runtime. Accounting is seeded with Avilo's AI posture. The policy is **read by the engine**, not merely displayed.
- Prototype test: on Accounting, the Governance Section renders the seeded entries; the user adds a `deny` entry and an action that entry forbids is refused at runtime with the entry named as the reason — proven by attempting the action, not by reading the config back. On a Module with no entries the Section still renders with an honest empty state (present-not-absent). Both checked live in the running app.
- Scope: Stage 3 of [`docs/raw/module-merge-accounting-d2c-2026-08-16.md`](raw/module-merge-accounting-d2c-2026-08-16.md) §6. New `ModuleGovernanceSection.tsx` in `ModuleSurfaceLayout`'s `below` slot after `ModuleIntelligenceSection`; `governance` block added to `ModuleManifest` and validated at the `apps/api` seam like every other manifest field; Accounting's seed per plan §7b.
- Evidence: Avilo BUG-029 through BUG-045 — the assistant claiming work it had not done, repeatedly, under a rule that existed only in prose. Avilo ADR-045 records prompt text failing twice on the same defect before the approach changed.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-16, verbatim: *"Bring in the governance section to all modules below the intelligence section where user can explicitly add whats allowed and whats not. In this, add Avilos specific rules for avilo module"*
- Approval: AP-163 APPLIED (ADR-248)
- Dependencies: TASK-074
- The exit test is enforcement, not rendering. A Governance Section that only displays policy reproduces exactly the prose-nobody-enforces failure ADR-248 exists to end, and would close this task falsely.
- Landed 2026-08-16: `governance` block on `ModuleManifest` with loud validation (a rule with no `reason` is rejected — a rule that cannot explain itself cannot be audited, and "empty" is the *permissive* state so a corrupt policy must never install as empty); `governanceVerdict`/`assertModuleGovernance` in `@bridge/core` with deny-always-wins and segment-prefix matching (`model` matches `model.call`, never `models.list`; regex rejected outright); Accounting seeded with Avilo's posture as data; `ModuleGovernanceSection.tsx` wired below Intelligence at all 10 call sites. 8/8 new tests pass.
- Landed 2026-08-16 (part 2): `accounting.overrides.create` (`router.ts`) is the governed call site — it takes `actor: { type: "human" | "model", id }`, resolves `action` to `books.write.model` or `books.write.human`, and calls `assertModuleGovernance("accounting", accountingManifest.governance, action)` before the insert. A `"model"` actor hits the manifest's seeded `books.write.model` deny rule and the mutation throws `TRPCError({ code: "FORBIDDEN" })` quoting that rule's `reason`; a `"human"` actor's call proceeds — no synthetic client/row is created to force the call to succeed. Live-verified: Accounting's Governance Section renders "Allowed 2 / Denied 3" with the seeded rules and reasons visible; D2C (no `governance` declared) renders "Allowed 0 / Denied 0" with the honest "an empty policy is not a default-deny" copy rather than hiding the section — both checked in a running dev server, not by reading the manifest back.

A governed listing crawler obeys each site's robots.txt before every request, the per-Source rights/pause/spend gate runs before any connector is touched, findings are ranked by Thesis fit, and each Source can be switched on or off from the Sources page.
- Prototype test: on the Sources page all 20 Sources are listed with an "On" checkbox; the three bot-protected Sources (BizBuySell, Premier Business Brokers, Sunbelt) render blocked and unchecked with spendCap 0, and the two account-gated ones (APS, Kumo) render paused. Unchecking a Source pauses it and `dealpilot.runDiscovery` then reports it skipped with reason `source_paused` rather than crawling it. A Source left `unattested` is skipped with `rights_required`. Adding a new Source through the existing create Form and attesting it makes it eligible on the next run.
- Scope: ADR-254; AP-164. New `platform/packages/sourcing/src/robots.ts` (RFC 9309 parser + `isPathAllowed`) and `src/connectors/listing-crawler.ts` (`createListingCrawlerConnector`, `robotsRefusalFor`); new `platform/modules/dealpilot/src/source-catalog.ts` (20 Sources with per-site robots evidence + `stripTrackingParams`), `src/listing-extract.ts` (JSON-LD then card heuristic), `src/discovery.ts` (`runSourceDiscovery`), `src/text-fields.ts` (helpers lifted out of `connectors.ts`, behaviour unchanged); virtual `enabled` column on the sources page manifest in `src/domain.ts`; catalog seeding + `createDealPilotListingCrawler` over `guardedFetch` in `platform/apps/api/src/wiring.ts`; `dealpilot.runDiscovery` in `platform/apps/api/src/router.ts`; `dealpilot.runDiscovery` classified Local-Plane-only in `src/deployment-boundary.ts`; enable checkbox wiring in `platform/apps/web/src/app/pages/DealPilotPage.tsx`.
- Evidence: `@bridge/sourcing` 38/38 (18 robots + 13 crawler new); `@bridge/dealpilot` 140/140 (15 catalog + 18 extraction + 12 discovery new; the 22 pre-existing connector tests still green after the `text-fields.ts` extraction); `@bridge/api` 521/521 incl. `procedure-classification` and `public-cloud-boundary`; monorepo `pnpm -r typecheck` clean. Removal-fails PERFORMED and seen red on 11 behaviours across robots matching, refusal semantics, crawl pacing, the trust tag, the discovery gate, and both extraction fabrication guards. robots.txt for all 20 domains fetched and read 2026-08-17; postures recorded per entry in `source-catalog.ts`. LIMIT: no live crawl has been run from the running app — the capability is proven by test, not by a real fetch against a real broker site.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-16/17, verbatim: *"I want you to integrate a crawler or whatever to go through these links for deal pilot for the investor to know about the current deals going on and suggest the best ones. SOme might need login, Bridge should ask for login for the user to get the details and also he should have an option to check or uncheck any of these sources or even add new ones"* plus a 20-row source table; continued on *"continue"*; shipped on *"Push and merge all the changes"*.
- Approval: AP-164 applied
- Dependencies: TASK-032

## Fresh-machine reproduction — the desktop bundle could not build or boot from a clean clone

- [x] TASK-078 — Reproduce the colleague's fresh-install failure and repair the desktop bundle (2026-08-27; ADR-256, AP-166)
  - Wiped the machine to a colleague-equivalent state: app + all data/caches removed (user data backed up to session scratchpad first), `main` pulled to 4881f600, every `node_modules`, the 11GB cargo `target`, `dist` and `generated` deleted.
  - Found three independent fresh-build breakages, in the order a fresh machine hits them: (1) `prepare:bundle` refuses better-sqlite3's prebuilds + @napi-rs/canvas's skia `.node` in Resources (in since ac6de169); (2) the build then exits 1 demanding the CI-only `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` (in since TASK-077); (3) the built app boots a dead API — `migrations-accounting/` missing from the deploy because of `apps/api`'s `files` whitelist, sidecar dies before reporting its port.
  - Fixed: keyring-pattern Framework + dlopen loader for better-sqlite3 (`BRIDGE_SQLITE3_NATIVE_LIBRARY`, fail-closed in release), canvas pruned (optional by design, text-only pdf use), `updaterArtifactsConfig()` skips updater artifacts when the private key is absent, `migrations-accounting` added to the `files` whitelist + `verifyInputs` requiredPaths.
  - Evidence: bundle policy tests 11/11 (3 new, each seen red first); `pnpm build:tauri` exit 0 from the scratch state; in-bundle positive/negative sqlite loader proof (`{"x":42}` / named fail-closed error); 0 `.node` in Resources; fresh launch → `api sidecar healthy`, accounting migrations ran, webview mutation 200, three windows up. `cargo test api_sidecar` green (see ADR-256 verified block for the count).
  - Not verified: first-run model download had not begun within the observation window; window contents not screenshotted (user was actively working in another Space).
