## UI canon repair — every rule states what to build, and Module Detail leaves the docs
- ID: TASK-080
- Status: done (2026-08-30)
- Priority: P1
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: `docs/wiki/ui-architecture.md` contains no reference to Module Detail, no rule phrased as the negation of a past mistake, and no rule whose content is "these examples are not the definition". The data-shape tree is axed on the Database per ADR-259 (one Database → LIST whatever changes; Databases sharing Relations → TOGGLE; conceptually related Modules → sub-modules; unrelated → new Module), and `docs/wiki/ui-rulebook.md` exists as the single consolidated reading surface. Add-row, Form, Module and Agent each carry a positive definition; DealPilot, JobPilot and CoS appear only as illustrations after a definition, never in place of one. `CLAUDE.md` and this file say "manifest" where they said `module.yaml`.
- Prototype test: no line in `docs/wiki/ui-architecture.md` directs a reader to Module Detail as a live surface — the only surviving mention states that it was deleted and that admin therefore has no home, which is the note that stops it being re-added. `CLAUDE.md` contains no `module.yaml`; the only occurrences left in this file are inside this task, describing the fix. A reader who has never seen the app can state what New does and what a Form view does from the doc alone, without inferring either from what the other is not.
- Scope: `docs/wiki/ui-architecture.md`, `docs/wiki/ui-rulebook.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/TASKS.md` (TASK-074 scope line).
- Evidence: Module Detail deleted 2026-08-10 (`platform/apps/web/src/app/routes.tsx:167-175`, redirect only) yet referenced 4× in `ui-architecture.md`. `find . -name module.yaml` outside node_modules returns zero files; manifests are `platform/modules/manifests/src/index.ts`.
- Requests: user directives 2026-08-29 (AP-168), verbatim: *"I dont want any such rules, Ensure the underlying idea is strongly enforced through clear rules than sharing rules like this."*; *"Can you ensure Module detail is deleted and not referred anywhere in rule book and take corrective measures?"*
- Approval: AP-168 APPLIED (ADR-258)
- Dependencies: none
- LANDED 2026-08-30: Module Detail purged from both the wiki summary and the deep rules doc (the wiki-only fix was incomplete — the raw §4b was still a full section instructing readers to use it); `module.yaml` corrected to the manifest path in `CLAUDE.md` and this file; the two rejected meta-rules deleted; positive definitions written for Module, sub-module, Agent, View, List, New, Form view and element page; the data-shape tree re-axed on the Database (ADR-259). The consolidation went further than this task's original scope: ADR-260 made `docs/raw/ui-rulebook.md` the ONE canon and retired four competing documents to stubs, and ADR-261 closed all six open decisions. Enforcement now exists at four layers (`docs/wiki/ui-enforcement.md`) — the `no-live-module-detail` doc gate is what keeps this task from regressing.
- The OPEN QUESTION this task carried — admin having no surface — is RESOLVED by ADR-261/AP-171 and owned by TASK-089: admin is Organization ownership and membership, behind the rail's Organization control, with Module mount/unmount/scopes/versions inside it. Module Detail is not revived.
## Left-nav Modules become directly manageable — right-click menu and drag-reorder
- ID: TASK-081
- Status: done (2026-09-02, AP-176/ADR-270 — the Files-folder half now lands too; see Landed below)
- Priority: P2
- Estimate: 1d
- Horizon: Hardening
- Outcome: right-clicking a Module in the rail opens a context menu offering **Hide** and **Rename**, the same gesture that opens the column menu on a table header; hidden Modules are restored from a rail **View options** entry and are never lost; Modules are drag-reorderable and the order persists per Organization.
- Prototype test: right-click a Module → menu opens with Hide and Rename; Hide removes it from the rail and it reappears in View options as unchecked; re-checking restores it in its previous position. Drag a Module above another, reload the app, and the new order survives. A renamed Module's local file folder follows the rename (ADR-178's carry-forward path), verified by listing `~/Documents/Bridge/<Org>/`.
- Scope: `platform/apps/web/src/app/Layout.tsx` (`renderModuleLink`, `renderMobileModuleLink`). `ListPillRow.tsx`/`Pill.tsx`'s `onPillContextMenu` is the nearest existing nav-style right-click precedent; `StandardColumnMenu`'s hide/View-options pairing is the interaction to mirror rather than reinvent.
- Evidence: `grep -n "onContextMenu" Layout.tsx` returns nothing; no `draggable`/`onDragStart`/`onDrop` anywhere in the file. Order is computed — `DEFAULT_MODULES`, then `modules.list` filtered to available, then `buildModuleNavTree` — with no ordering key persisted. Only `bridge.<org>.rail.expandedModules.v1` persists today.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-29 (AP-168), verbatim: *"Right click a module to hide it and similar to view option for columns right clicking on left nav bar should should view options. Also I should be able to drag and rearrange modules. On right click of A module, also provide rename option."*
- Approval: AP-168 APPLIED (ADR-258)
- Dependencies: none
- Constraint: hiding is a rail-presentation change only. It never uninstalls a Module, never alters permissions or plane, and never filters an installed Module out of Intelligence or search — grouping is a re-arrangement, never a filter (ADR-178).
- LANDED 2026-08-30: right-click menu (Hide / Rename) on rail Modules, rail **View options** restoring hidden Modules in their previous position, and HTML5 drag-reorder. Presentation persists per Organization at `bridge.<org>.rail.modulePresentation.v1` via `platform/apps/web/src/app/rail-module-presentation.ts` (10 unit tests, `test/rail-module-presentation.test.mjs`). Drag payload rides `dataTransfer` (MIME `application/x-bridge-rail-module`), not React state — an `onDragStart` `setState` and the `onDrop` that reads it land in the same batch, so the state path read `null`.
- LANDED 2026-09-02 (ADR-270), closing the last clause of the Prototype test: **a renamed Module's local Files folder now follows the rename.** `module_installations.display_name_override` (migration 0046, nullable TEXT) carries what an Organization calls a Module, written by a new `modules.rename` mutation that moves the folder in the same call. Set on EVERY version row for that (Organization, Module), so promote/rollback cannot lose the name. The ten hand-spelled `manifest.module.displayName ?? moduleName` folder-label expressions collapse into one `moduleFolderLabel(installation)` — two sites that disagree would put a Module's Files in two directories and the one nobody is looking at reads as empty. `renameModuleFolder` reuses `adoptRenamedModuleFolder`'s conservative shape: acts only when the source is a real directory and the destination does not exist, so it never merges or overwrites, and re-running is a no-op. When a folder already occupies the new name the rename still lands and the move does not (`destination-exists`) — a label is recoverable, a merged directory is not. Two new api tests: the full round trip (rename → folder moved with the owner's file inside it → `modules.files` reads the new root → rename back clears the override and moves it back), and the collision case. The round-trip test was seen failing first, against a `moduleFolderLabel` that ignored the override: `actual .../DealManager, expected .../Pipeline`. Green: `@bridge/api` `modules` 27/27, `@bridge/core` suite, `@bridge/db` `migration-metadata` (its `drizzle-kit generate` no-op assertion again proves the 0046 snapshot), `@bridge/web` build. **Superseded by this:** the paragraph below, kept for the record.
- SUPERSEDED 2026-09-02, kept as written: **a renamed Module's local Files folder does not follow the rename.** The rename is client-persisted presentation; the folder path is derived server-side from the Module's canonical name. Making the folder follow needs a per-Organization `displayNameOverride` on the installation record plus a folder-migration path — schema plus persistence, which is Tier C and needs its own approval. Until then rename is a rail label only. This is the unmet half of the Prototype test above; TASK-081 stays `in_progress` rather than being marked done against a test it half-passes.
- ALSO NOT VERIFIED at runtime: Enter/Escape on the rename input, and drag-reorder on the mobile drawer (mobile has no drag affordance — desktop rail only).

## Chat composer — the paperclip uploads, and the mic works everywhere
- ID: TASK-082
- Status: in_progress (uploads + cross-surface mic LANDED 2026-08-30, ADR-263; the Prototype test's live web-session runs are outstanding — see below)
- Priority: P2
- Estimate: 0.5d
- Horizon: Hardening
- Outcome: the chat paperclip accepts a file and routes it through the same Module File path every other upload uses, landing under `~/Documents/Bridge/<Org>/<Module>/`; the chat mic records and transcribes on **every** Bridge surface — desktop, web and mobile — rather than only inside the Tauri shell.
- Prototype test: attach a file in a web (non-Tauri) browser session, then find that exact file on disk under the Module's folder and see it referenced from the message. Press the mic in a web session and get a transcript in the composer, with no `__TAURI_INTERNALS__` present in that context.
- Scope: `platform/apps/web/src/app/chat/ChatView.tsx` (`isDesktopShell`, the disabled paperclip button at ~L965, the `companion_transcribe` call at ~L566); a server-side transcription procedure in `platform/apps/api/src/router.ts`; `modules.addFile` is the existing upload path and must be reused, not duplicated.
- Evidence: the paperclip is hardcoded `disabled` with title "Attachments aren't supported yet — this Chat doesn't have an upload pipeline"; the mic is gated on `window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__?.invoke` because `companion_transcribe` is a desktop-only Rust command (`platform/apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/companion.rs`). `modules.addFile` already exists at `router.ts:19442`. No `whisper`/`stt` route exists server-side today.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-29 (AP-168), verbatim: *"Instead of disabling paper clips enable uploads."*; *"the mic in chatbot should be available in all bridge versions"*
- Approval: AP-168 APPLIED (ADR-258)
- Dependencies: none
- Not in scope, and deliberately so: the panel Chat mic keeps filling the draft for human review rather than auto-sending. The **Avatar** shortcut already auto-sends (`CompanionAsk.tsx` calls `ask(transcript)` directly) and that behaviour is correct and unchanged; the panel differs because a Chat turn can start governed Task proposals. AP-168 does not approve collapsing that distinction.
- LANDED 2026-08-30: the paperclip uploads through **`trpc.modules.addFile`** — the one existing Module File path, not a second storage route — and the mic runs on every surface via a new `chat.voice.transcribe` mutation backed by `platform/apps/api/src/voice-transcription.ts` (Groq `whisper-large-v3-turbo`, the same model, container allow-list and 24 MB ceiling as the desktop Rust command). `isDesktopShell`, `companion_capabilities` and `companion_transcribe` are gone from the panel composer. Audio is decoded, forwarded once, never persisted; the key rides one `Authorization` header and the fetch-failure branch deliberately drops the cause, which would carry it.
- The desktop `companion_transcribe` Rust command STAYS — `CompanionAsk.tsx` still uses it, and the Avatar shortcut still auto-sends. Only ChatView stopped depending on the Tauri shell. The panel/Avatar distinction this row protects is pinned by test.
- Residency: transcription audio is raw capture, so `chat.voice.` is denied in public-cloud; attachments ride `modules.addFile`, already Local-Plane-only. Both refusals are reported **ahead of time** through `chat.model.status.composer` (`{available, reason}` per control), so the control is visible-and-disabled carrying the server's own reason (ADR-001) rather than failing after the user has recorded or picked a file.
- Attachment target Module is `task-manager` — Chief of Staff's own Module, and a default Module, so it is always installed. Files land at `~/Documents/Bridge/<Org>/TaskManager/`.
- CEILING, named rather than hidden: **a Chat turn is plain text and has no attachment column.** The saved path is appended to the draft as `[Attachment: Bridge/TaskManager/<file>]`, so the sent message carries the reference as text. Structured attachments need a `chat_turn_attachments` table plus `chatSendInput.attachments` — a migration, and therefore its own Tier C row.
- NOT DONE: the Prototype test above specifies **live web-session runs** — attach a file in a non-Tauri browser and find that exact file on disk; press the mic in a web session and get a transcript with no `__TAURI_INTERNALS__` present. Neither has been watched. Both paths are unit-tested red-first and the bundle is clean, but this row is not done until a human has seen both in a browser.

## Creation is one gesture — New opens an element page, and ⋮ gains per-element sections
- ID: TASK-083
- Status: done (2026-09-03, LANDED — see LANDED/NOT LANDED below)
- Priority: P1
- Estimate: 3d
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: on every surface, the New affordance opens a new element page showing all of that Database's fields — identical to that element's page as it appears before any user input, with column defaults already applied; nothing is written until Save (C-34; defaults clarified ADR-259/AP-169 — pre-filling a default is not a write). The toolbar ⋮ gains an **Elements** entry offering **per-DATABASE** toggles for **Notes**, **Intelligence** and **Governance** (resolved ADR-261/AP-171): switching one on or off applies to every element of that Database. Per-element toggling is rejected — two Records of one Database with different Sections is the single-page divergence the gate exists to catch.
- Prototype test: on a table with a `formula` column, a relation column and a column carrying a default, New opens an element page listing every field including those, with the default already shown and every other field empty; backing out without Save leaves no row and no partial record. Enable Notes from ⋮ → Elements, reopen ANY element page of that Database, and the Notes Section is there on all of them; disable it and it is gone from all of them, with existing notes preserved rather than deleted.
- Scope: `platform/apps/web/src/app/dataviews/views/TableView.tsx` (the draft-row path), `DataViews.tsx` (the ⋮ at L416-532), the per-Module Record Detail pages. **The gated test `"the add-row belongs to the table's shape, not to a per-page opt-in"` (`ui-conformance.test.mjs:292`) must be REWRITTEN, not deleted** — it must still assert the New affordance is always present and states a reason when disabled, which is the defect it was written for (JobPilot and Signals silently lost a control DealPilot had).
- Evidence: the draft-row inline-create path is live in `TableView.tsx`; `DataViews.tsx`'s ⋮ currently offers only Add column, View options, Sort by, Reset view.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-29 (AP-168), verbatim: *"Replace [the add-row rule] so that add row takes to a new elements page with all fields shown."*; *"Under 3 dots, add elements option inside with provide option to enable notes, intelligence and governance per element."*
- Approval: AP-168 APPLIED (ADR-258)
- Dependencies: none
- REVERSAL, recorded so it is not mistaken for drift: this overrides **C-33** ("table-shaped records are created in the table"), which ADR-249 had used to reconcile C-7 with Bridge's inline add-row. C-7 now applies universally. The zero-rows rule is untouched — the table keeps full chrome and says nothing at zero rows.
- LANDED 2026-09-03: the draft row is GONE from `TableView.tsx` (state, commit path, in-place row and `DraftCell` all deleted). New raises `onRequestCreate`; `<DataViews>` owns the page (`dataviews/RecordPage.tsx`), so the table and the Form view cannot grow two different create surfaces again. The page lists EVERY column — a `formula`, `skill` or locked column is shown with what fills it rather than dropped — pre-fills `defaultValue` into React state (ADR-259: pre-filling a default is not a write), and issues exactly one request, on Save. The ⋮ gained its per-DATABASE Sections entry over `records.sections/setSection` (human-only, membership-checked, Local-Plane state at `record:sections:<specId>`), with notes at `record:notes:<specId>` — a SEPARATE namespace, which is what makes switching Notes off a hide rather than a delete. `RecordSections.tsx` is the one component both the new-Record page and `TaskRecordDetailPage` render, so the toggle cannot mean one thing on one page and another elsewhere.
- VOCABULARY: the directive's word "Elements" is retired canon (glossary: Record). Every identifier, API and label ships as **Record** — the ⋮ entry reads **Records** — because `check:vocabulary` is the authority on copy and an allowlist entry for a brand-new surface would be grandfathering, not migration. The behaviour AP-171 approved is unchanged.
- The gated test was REWRITTEN, not deleted: `"New is always present, states a reason when disabled, and opens the Record page"` still asserts the control never vanishes and still states a reason (the JobPilot/Signals defect), and now also asserts no draft row survives. Two more conformance tests came with it — every field on the page, and per-DATABASE keying with no Record-scoped variant to diverge.
- NOT LANDED: only ONE per-Module Record Detail page exists in the repository (`TaskRecordDetailPage`), so "reopen ANY Record page of that Database" is provable on Tasks and nowhere else — every other surface opens a Record in a side panel. The Sections toggle is real and per-Database; the second Record-page surface to prove it against does not exist yet. No browser verification (TASK-041).
- Evidence 2026-09-03: api `record-sections` 5/5 over the real wiring (default off, per-Database keying, Agent refused, notes preserved across a disable, corrupt state reads as all-off); web suite 220/220 including the three new conformance tests; `check:vocabulary` and `check:ui-rules` clean; both typechecks clean.
- CAUGHT BY THE FULL API SUITE, not by the targeted run: `records.*` was **unclassified** at the public-cloud boundary, and `procedure-classification` failed the build naming it — exactly what that gate exists for. Now closed with a reason (Local-Plane state; a Record note is user content the Local Plane holds). The other three failures in that run were a stale `dist/test/element-sections.test.js` left behind by the rename — see BUGS.md.

## A governed schema-mutation capability, so the column menu stops lying
- ID: TASK-084
- Status: in_progress (capability + 9 live menu items + fx LANDED 2026-08-30, ADR-264; add/duplicate column stay disabled with a stated reason, and the recompute clause is only half-runnable — see below)
- Priority: P2
- Estimate: 2d
- Horizon: Hardening
- Outcome: a governed schema-mutation capability exists, and `StandardColumnMenu`'s items enable against it. Commands the surface can run are **enabled and warn** — stating the consequence and proceeding on confirmation — rather than refusing; only genuinely impossible commands stay disabled with a reason. A `formula` cell's editor carries an **fx** affordance that toggles between editing the value and editing the expression, binding to Accounting's existing formula engine rather than growing a second one.
- Prototype test: Rename a column and see the change persist and survive reload. Delete a column and get a dependency preview naming the Views, Automations, Skills, formulas and Relations that break, with a working undo. Open a formula cell, click fx, edit the expression, and see the validator reject a bad expression before it commits and every dependent cell recompute after a good one. No menu item is enabled that fails at the server.
- Scope: `platform/apps/web/src/app/components/shared/StandardColumnMenu.tsx` (13 of 17 items permanently disabled today), the schema-mutation procedures in `platform/apps/api/src/router.ts`, `platform/packages/tables/src/types.ts` (`ColumnKind` already includes `"formula"`), and `platform/modules/accounting/src/formulas/{registry,engine}.ts` — `engine.ts:510` already validates a candidate expression before save and is the seam to reuse.
- Evidence: 13 of 17 column-menu items carry one of two disabled reasons — "this surface has no governed schema-mutation capability" or "dependency preview and undo are required". Live today: Filter, Sort ascending, Sort descending, Hide column. No fx glyph exists anywhere in the repository; no formula editor exists in the web app; `formula` columns are excluded from Form view as read-only (`FormView.tsx:44`).
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-29 (AP-168), verbatim: *"Replace unsupported writes disabled with reason with enabled with warning"*; and the fx/formula-toggle behaviour described in the same turn.
- Approval: AP-168 APPLIED (ADR-258)
- Dependencies: none — but the enabled-with-warning half of the outcome cannot land before the capability exists. Enabling the 13 items first would ship a menu that looks live and fails at the server, which ADR-247 ("the server has the last word"; never claim what isn't true) forbids. ADR-001 (present-not-absent) is unchanged: nothing is ever hidden, at any stage.
- LANDED 2026-08-30: `tableSchema.{get,preview,mutate,undo,setFormulaExpression}` over a per-Organization **column overlay** on the Local Plane (`table:schema:<specId>`), resolved over the shipped `TableSpec` by `applyColumnOverlay` (`platform/packages/tables`). Human-only, membership-checked, closed to the public-cloud shell. `platform/apps/api/src/table-schema.ts` holds the rules; `FormulaCellEditor.tsx` is the fx affordance. `@bridge/tables` became an API dependency so the overlay has exactly one implementation.
- THE 17-ITEM SPLIT, honestly: **9 live** — Filter, Sort ascending, Sort descending, Hide column (unchanged) plus Rename, Edit column, Change type, Lock/Unlock, Delete column (new, enabled-and-warn) — plus an 18th, Undo last column change. **8 visible and disabled with a stated reason**: AI Smartfill (no Smartfill capability installed), Group (not wired for this View — TASK-087 owns it), Calculate (the table footer already does it), **Add column left / right / Duplicate column** (the real ceiling, below), Add page, Remove page. All 17 labels remain in the source and the gate asserts no `disabled` appears without a reason.
- THE CEILING that keeps 3 items disabled: **the overlay describes columns the Database already has.** A newly added column has nowhere to store its values, so enabling Add/Duplicate would ship precisely the menu-that-fails-at-the-server this row forbids. Closing it needs a cell-value store or real per-Organization migrations — its own Tier C row.
- Enabled-and-warn uses a Bridge `<Dialog>`, never `window.confirm` (a counted gate violation, and a native dialog cannot carry the dependency preview). Change type uses `<Select>`, not a bare `<select>`; Rename uses `<Input>`, not `prompt`.
- The dependency preview distinguishes **"inspected, none found"** from **"never looked"**. Views: inspected, none possible (no `ViewConfig` is persisted anywhere — every surface rebuilds from `defaultViewConfig`). Formulas and Relations: inspected, real. **Automations and Skills: `inspected: false`** — `automationStep` carries opaque `inputs` with no column-level reference in the contract, so a step naming a column cannot be found. Rendered as not-inspected in danger colour. An empty list from an uninspected source is not safety.
- fx binds to the EXISTING engine: `engine.ts`'s `validateExpression` is called server-side before the write, and on refusal the editor stays open showing the validator's own words. The web app never parses or evaluates — a conformance assertion forbids `mathjs`/`parse(`/`evaluate(` in the editor. `dependents` comes from the engine's own graph.
- NOT FULLY RUNNABLE: the Prototype test's *"every dependent cell recompute"* clause. The recompute path is real and derived, but with no imported P&L facts every metric's value is `null` and renders as an honest empty cell. **No figure was fabricated to make the clause demonstrable** (ADR-247). You will see the expression change and the dependents named; you will not see numbers move until facts are imported. Consequence: Accounting → Reports' new Value column reads blank in every row today.
- SCOPE OF THE CAPABILITY: 6 tables (Accounting Clients/Reports, D2C Orders/Inventory/Research/Notes) — the specs the server holds. Every other table reports `available: false` **with a reason** and the menu disables against that answer, rather than assuming mutability. Extending it means registering more shipped specs server-side.
- NOT DONE: no browser verification; the value side of the fx toggle is read-only on Reports (editing a formula's value is a metric override needing a `(client, period)` scope that Page does not have — stated inline in the editor); `FormView.tsx:44` still excludes formula columns.

## Triage the cross-repository UI rule intake — adopt, scope, or reject each candidate
- ID: TASK-085
- Status: done (2026-09-02, AP-175/ADR-269)
- Priority: P2
- Estimate: 1d
- Horizon: Hardening
- Outcome: every candidate in [`docs/raw/ui-rule-intake-cross-repo-2026-08-30.md`](raw/ui-rule-intake-cross-repo-2026-08-30.md) has a recorded disposition — adopted as Bridge canon, scoped to one Module, or rejected with a reason — and the adopted set is in `docs/wiki/ui-rulebook.md` and `docs/wiki/ui-architecture.md`. Category 0 needs no approval and lands first: those rules are ALREADY canon in `docs/raw/ui-architecture-rules-2026-07.md` and were lost when the wiki compressed 486 lines to 113.
- Prototype test: a reader of `docs/wiki/ui-rulebook.md` alone can state the Notes Section and Record Governance Section requirements, the canonical toolbar slot order, the Secret Fields rule and the keyboard-parity rule — none of which the rulebook carried before this task. No candidate sits undisposed; a rejected candidate names why.
- Scope: `docs/wiki/ui-rulebook.md`, `docs/wiki/ui-architecture.md`, and an APPROVALS row per adopted group. ADR-247's selection test governs: *does this rule change what an agent does on a Module it did not come from?* — not *is this rule good*. ADR-247 explicitly rejected promoting everything, because always-loaded instruction budget is a governed recurring cost; a hundred adopted rules would repeat exactly the failure it prevented.
- Evidence: 2026-09-02 — disposed on the adopt-on-divergence test; 13 of ~110 candidates outside Category 0 adopted, the rest COVERED/SCOPED/REJECTED with reasons in the intake doc, which moves to `status: triaged`. Exit test met: `docs/wiki/ui-rulebook.md` alone now states the Notes Section, the Record Governance Section, the canonical toolbar slot order, the Secret Fields rule and keyboard parity (`ui-architecture.md` is the ADR-260 stub, so the rulebook is the one place). Found while checking claims rather than asserting them: three of `check-ui-rules.mjs`'s eight counted gates enforced rules absent from canon — now written as C-23a (no native `confirm`/`prompt`/`alert`, 17 call sites held at baseline for TASK-073), C-14a (`dvh` never `vh`) and the Part I §3 overscroll clause. Green: `check-ui-rules.mjs` (3 doc gates, 388 baselined), its test 4/4, `ui-conformance.test.mjs` 15/15, `@bridge/web` build. Prior evidence: three parallel repository scans 2026-08-30 (Avilo dashboard v9, CV Naturals, Repo/Avatar + clicky-avatar) plus a re-read of Bridge's own raw rules doc. Verified against Bridge's code the same day — ABSENT: toolbar density ladder, any print/`.paper` mode, sparklines, `isRouteErrorResponse`, table multi-select, long-press in `TableView`. PRESENT and not to be re-adopted: the 220ms double-click grace, server-speaks-first, "already configured is not a refusal".
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-30, verbatim: *"Can you look for all UI rules across the projects and see if we are missing any rules. List them"*
- Approval: none needed for Category 0 (already canon, only unindexed). Categories 1–3 need an APPROVALS row per adopted group — this task does not pre-approve them.
- Dependencies: TASK-080 (canon repair) should land first so adopted rules are written in the positive-definition form ADR-258 requires.
- THREE items are corrections, not additions, and should be handled ahead of the rest:
  1. **C-12 is stale.** CV Naturals narrowed it on 2026-08-17 (their ADR-017/AP-023) — holding a row enters multi-select, not the context menu — one day after Bridge imported it. Bridge holds the superseded text.
  2. **`StandardDropdown` uses `absolute top-full`**, the construction CV Naturals replaced with viewport-fixed positioning after menus were clipped unreachable on short tables. Bridge's own conformance gate flags that pattern as a reinvention when a PAGE uses it, while the shared primitive is built on it.
  3. **`StandardDropdown`'s positioning is RESOLVED** (ADR-261/AP-171): menus position against the viewport (`position: fixed`), never absolutely inside a scroll container. Build it here.
  4. **`100vh` in five avatar surfaces against one `dvh` use.** CVN's rule is `dvh` never `vh`, because iPad Safari's collapsing bars make `vh` measure the largest viewport.

## Multi-select on every table, entered by long-press on touch
- ID: TASK-086
- Status: in_progress (selection, bulk delete and the governance equivalence LANDED 2026-08-30, ADR-264; the touch clauses of the Prototype test need a real device — see below)
- Priority: P2
- Estimate: 0.5d
- Horizon: Hardening
- Outcome: every table supports multi-select — a checkbox column and a "Delete N" action bar — entered on touch by long-pressing a row and on pointer by the checkbox. While multi-select is active a tap toggles the row rather than opening it. The row context menu keeps its touch path through the row caret (§5f: right-click ≡ caret ≡ `StandardRowMenu`), so no new affordance is invented. Where an element has no context menu, long-press still stands in for hover-reveal.
- Prototype test: on a touch device, long-press a row → checkboxes appear and that row is selected; tap two more rows → count reads 3 and none of them opened; the caret still opens the same menu right-click opens; Escape (or the bar's Cancel) clears selection. A bulk delete of 3 Records produces the same governance decisions as three single deletes — verified in the ledger, not by eye.
- Scope: `platform/apps/web/src/app/dataviews/views/TableView.tsx` (no long-press or selection state exists today — `grep onTouchStart` and `grep selectedRows` both return nothing), `StandardRowMenu`, and the bulk path in `router.ts`. The action bar is shared chrome, not per-page.
- Evidence: Bridge has zero multi-select and zero long-press handling. CV Naturals shipped this and narrowed C-12 for it on 2026-08-17 (their ADR-017/AP-023) — one day after Bridge imported the superseded text.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-30 (AP-171), verbatim: *"Bridge needs multiselect, long press on touch screen"*
- Approval: AP-171 APPLIED (ADR-261)
- Dependencies: none
- Constraint: a bulk action obeys the same governance as its single-Record form — never a second, thinner write path. The synthetic click that fires on finger-lift must be swallowed, or the long-press that opened selection also opens the row.
- LANDED 2026-08-30: a resident checkbox column on every table, long-press-to-select on touch, tap-toggles-while-selecting, Escape/Cancel to clear, and a shared `TableSelectionBar` with a real confirmation dialog. `relationship.archiveRecords` is the bulk server path.
- THE CONSTRAINT IS MET STRUCTURALLY, not by assertion. `onDeleteRows(ids: string[])` is the **only** delete prop — there is deliberately no singular one, so the row caret's Delete and the cell menu's Delete row both call it with a one-element list, and a test forbids a singular prop from appearing. At the server the same move: `archiveRelationshipRecord` was extracted and `archivePerson`, `archiveCommunity` **and** `archiveRecords` all call it. Proven red-first by temporarily giving the bulk loop its own inline `pipeline.propose` — the ledger test caught the second write path by its differing `action`/`skill`/`userDecision`.
- The gesture decisions live in a **pure reducer** (`selection.ts`), not in JSX handlers, because the web suite has no jsdom and no react-dom — a swallow implemented inside an `onClick` closure is behaviour nothing can fail on. The synthetic-click trap was proven red first, and the real failure mode was worse than the row also opening: the lift toggled the row straight back **off**, emptying selection so the next tap opened a Record.
- Selection is keyed by the stable Record id, not the row index (same reason the red-flag anchor is); a row with no stable id is not selectable and its checkbox says why. Bulk is capped at 50 ids — an unbounded list is an unbounded number of governed proposals in one request. A per-id failure is reported rather than thrown, so a partial bulk cannot leave the user unable to tell which Records survived.
- NOT VERIFIED, and it cannot be verified here: **every touch clause of the Prototype test.** What is proven is the reducer — that the event sequence a finger produces swallows, toggles and clears correctly. What is NOT proven is that the DOM handlers fire those events at the right moments: that `onTouchStart` on `<tr>` receives the touch through the cells, that 500ms feels right, that the 10px drift tolerance distinguishes a scroll from a hold, and that swallowing the React `onClick` is enough on iOS Safari. **This needs a human on a real touch device.** No synthetic event dispatch was substituted, because that would prove only that events can be dispatched.
- SCOPE LANDED: only `RelationshipPage` wires a real delete. Every other table shows the checkbox column and, on selection, a bar whose Delete is visible and disabled with a stated reason (ADR-001). Wiring the rest needs a governed delete per Database and was not in this row.
- NOT BUILT: no select-all header checkbox (not in the Outcome). FOLLOW-UP worth taking: the cell menu now carries two Delete items (`StandardRowMenuItems`' "Delete" and `StandardCellMenu`'s "Delete row"); both route to the same path so they cannot diverge, but the redundancy pre-dates this task and should collapse.

## Grouping on every table, and Databases that link by column
- ID: TASK-087
- Status: ready
- Priority: P2
- Estimate: 3d
- Horizon: Hardening
- Outcome: (a) `onGroup` is wired so C-9 holds — every table offers grouping from the ⋮, and a surface that genuinely cannot group says why rather than showing a permanently dead command. (b) A relation column names a target Database and the column it matches on, and **the target Database gains the reverse view of that link without anyone declaring it twice** — a link is declared once and read from either side.
- Prototype test: group a table by a Dropdown column → collapsible groups with per-group counts, persisted in the View like any other overlay. Then link Database A to Database B by a column, open B, and find A's rows reachable there without having declared anything in B; unlink from B and the link is gone in A. C-32 (editable from both ends) is satisfied by construction rather than by a second declaration.
- Scope: `StandardColumnMenu.tsx` (Group is `disabled={!onGroup}` and `TableView` passes `onGroup` from nobody, so it is disabled on every table in the app), `DataViews.tsx`, the View overlay shape in `platform/packages/tables`, and the relation column kind.
- Evidence: `grep onGroup` finds the prop threaded but never supplied. `ColumnKind` already includes `relation`, so the column type exists; the reverse-side read does not.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-30 (AP-171), verbatim: *"We need on group. Also similar to notion, we should be able to link databases by certain columns and access it from its related database"*
- Approval: AP-171 APPLIED (ADR-261)
- Dependencies: none
- Note: this is C-32's missing mechanism, not a new rule beside it. C-32 required links be symmetric; nothing said how one is made.

## Governance becomes editable — an engine-held overlay, never a mutated manifest
- ID: TASK-088
- Status: in_progress (engine overlay + in-place editor LANDED and ENFORCED 2026-08-30, ADR-263; live browser verification and the second-Organization half of the Prototype test are outstanding — see below)
- Priority: P1
- Estimate: 1d
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: the effective governance policy for a Module is an **engine-held overlay keyed by Organization + Module**, resolved over the manifest's declared default. The engine enforces against the resolved policy; editing writes the overlay and sets `userEdited`; the manifest is never mutated. The Governance Section's edit affordance reaches a real editor instead of a link to a deleted page.
- Prototype test: edit Accounting's deny list in one Organization → enforcement changes for that Organization only, the manifest file on disk is byte-identical, the Section shows the "edited by you" state, and a second Organization still sees the shipped default. Reset the overlay → the declared default returns. A Module shipping no policy still renders the honest empty state, and an empty policy is still not a default-deny.
- Scope: a governance-overlay store (`platform/packages/core/src/module/governance.ts` holds `governanceVerdict`/`assertModuleGovernance` today; only one production caller exists, `router.ts:18575`, Accounting), the tRPC mutation, and `ModuleGovernanceSection.tsx`'s edit path. `module.governance.userEdited` is already parsed in `manifest.ts:529-537` and read by the UI — this task is what finally sets it.
- Evidence: the field exists and nothing in the codebase can write it; the Section's "Edit in Module Detail" link resolves to a redirect back to the page the user was already on (BUGS 2026-08-29). Manifests are immutable (ADR-178), so an editable manifest field was never possible — the flag was written for an overlay nobody built.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-30 (AP-171), verbatim: *"governance should be editable. It wasnt a module but an engine right?"*
- Approval: AP-171 APPLIED (ADR-261)
- Dependencies: none
- This is an ENGINE task, not a UI task. The correction in the directive is the substance: governance was already "engine-read" (ADR-248) while the edit affordance was written as though the Module owned the policy. Closes the BUGS 2026-08-29 dead-end link.
- LANDED 2026-08-30: `ModuleGovernanceOverlay` / `readModuleGovernanceOverlay` / `resolveModuleGovernance` in `platform/packages/core/src/module/governance.ts` (`governanceVerdict`/`assertModuleGovernance` untouched — the overlay resolves *into* them); `moduleGovernance.get/set/reset` in `router.ts`; the `accounting.overrides.create` call site now asserts against the **resolved** policy, not the manifest; `ModuleGovernanceSection.tsx`'s dead "Edit in Module Detail" link is replaced by an in-place editor with Save / Cancel / Restore declared. **Closes the BUGS 2026-08-29 dead-end link.** Persistence reuses the organization-scoped `LocalStateStore` (namespace `module:governance:<moduleName>`) — the same mechanism the learning-consent and WhatsApp-automation policies ride. No new store, no sqlite table, no migration.
- DECIDED, and it is a reading of "resolved over" rather than a literal one: **the overlay REPLACES the declared policy; it does not merge with it.** A merge leaves the user deleting a manifest deny and watching it come back. `reset` is how the declared default returns, and it is the only way. Recorded in ADR-263 so the next reader knows this was chosen, not assumed.
- Two guards added beyond this row's brief, both correct: `assertHumanIdentity` on `set`/`reset` (a rule an Agent can rewrite is not a boundary) and `assertKnownModule` (a typo'd name would store an overlay nothing reads while the user believed they had governed something). One fail-open was caught in review before it shipped: `{}` parsed as a *valid empty* overlay, which would have silently deleted the manifest's deny rules — `readRules` now rejects a missing list.
- Deployment boundary: `moduleGovernance.` is closed to the public cloud shell. Governance is Local-Plane state, and editing it moves the trust boundary.
- COVERAGE ADDED CENTRALLY at merge, because the overlay's own suite left two of this row's Prototype-test clauses unproven: `reset restores the declared default and clears userEdited` (proven to bite — mutating `reset` to `(current) => ({ state: current })` fails it) and `the overlay is keyed by Organization + Module` (proven at the store, one read returning a value and another returning `null` in the same run).
- NOT MET, and the reason: **"a second Organization still sees the shipped default" cannot be driven end to end.** Every `moduleGovernance` procedure calls `assertPilotOrganization` — the whole router is single-tenant-pinned, so a non-pilot Organization is *refused*, not served the default. That pin is asserted in the test rather than worked around; the clause unblocks when TASK-089 lands multi-Organization membership. This is a pre-existing platform constraint, not a defect in this work.
- NOT DONE: **no live browser verification.** It needs the API sidecar plus an authed session. The component typechecks, bundles and passes conformance, but nobody has watched a human save a policy. Tier C wants that seen; it stays outstanding and TASK-088 is not marked done without it.
- NOT BUILT: no overlay for non-built-in Modules. `declaredModuleGovernance` reads `BUILT_IN_MODULES`, matching the existing Accounting call site. Commons-attached Modules would need a store lookup; nothing needs it yet.

## Admin is Organization ownership and membership
- ID: TASK-089
- Status: in_progress (admin surface LANDED 2026-08-30, ADR-262; the invite/accept and mount/unmount half of the exit test is NOT met — see below)
- Priority: P2
- Estimate: 3d
- Horizon: Hardening
- Outcome: a user is admin of their own home Organization and a member of any number of others by invitation. The surface sits behind the **Organization control at the top of the rail** — the one slot ADR-180's closed left-nav scope already grants it. Module-scoped administration (mount/unmount, scopes, versions) lives inside it, scoped to the Organization, listing that Organization's installed Modules.
- Prototype test: from the Organization control, invite a second user to an Organization, accept as that user, and see both Organizations in their switcher with the correct role in each; mount and unmount a Module in one Organization and confirm the other is unaffected. A non-member cannot reach either.
- Scope: the rail's Organization control (`Layout.tsx`), an admin surface behind it, and the existing Organization-membership procedures in `router.ts` (SEC-6 scoped membership checks already exist; AP-014).
- Evidence: ADR-180 moved admin into Module Detail and ADR-224 deleted that page, leaving mount/unmount, scopes and versions with no surface at all — recorded as the open gap on TASK-080. The glossary already defines Organization as "user or team security, membership, billing, and data boundary" and Module Installation as an "Organization-scoped record", so the data shape this needs exists.
- Requests: user directive 2026-08-30 (AP-171), verbatim: *"Admin owns workspaces while a user is admin for his home workspace, he can be part of multiple workspaces based on where he's invited."*
- Approval: AP-171 APPLIED (ADR-261)
- Dependencies: none
- VOCABULARY: the directive says "workspace"; canon says **Organization** (ADR-171 retired `workspace`, and `check:vocabulary` would fail on it). Same concept, and the glossary definition matches the directive exactly. Do not reintroduce the retired noun in identifiers, copy, or routes.
- Admin is NOT a per-Module page. It is one Organization surface that lists the Modules installed in that Organization — reviving Module Detail to host it is explicitly rejected (ADR-261).
- LANDED 2026-08-30: `platform/apps/web/src/app/pages/OrganizationAdminPage.tsx` at route `/organization/admin`, reached from the rail's Organization control via a **Manage Organization** entry — the one slot ADR-180's closed rail scope grants it. Two tabs: **Modules** (that Organization's installed Modules) and **Members** (membership and role). Read-mostly.
- NOT LANDED, and the reason: **unmount is explained, not offered**, and there is no invite/accept flow. Both write against Organization-scoped installation and membership records; shipping either from a read-mostly surface without the server-side authority checks in place would be a governed write behind an ungoverned button. The exit test above — invite a second user, accept as that user, mount/unmount in one Organization and confirm the other is unaffected — is therefore NOT met.
- NOT VERIFIED: neither admin table has been seen with real rows. Honest empty states render; populated states are untested.

## Chat gets a swappable backend axis, and Claude Code answers turns in the panel and the Avatar

- ID: TASK-090
- Status: in_progress (landed 2026-09-02; the browser leg of the exit test is NOT met — see below)
- Priority: P1

- Outcome: a Chat thread names which ENGINE answers it as well as which plane its data may reach. The model menu in the Right Chat Panel and the Avatar composer offers Claude Code beside Local and Cloud; picking it starts a thread whose turns are answered by the Claude Agent SDK running headless in the API process — reading and editing files under `~/Documents/Bridge/<Organization>/`, with no terminal ever shown. Codex and Cursor become registry rows, not a second integration.

- Evidence: myzazoo (`Workspace/myzazoo/src/agents/claude.ts`, `src/serve.ts`) has run this exact shape in production for months; the user directed Bridge to match it.
- Requests: user directive 2026-09-02, verbatim: *"Refer myzazoo folder for builder agent reference and ensure we have the builder agent completely built and also similar to myzazoo, me having an option to choose claude as one of the model in the right hand chatbot or for avatar, while ensuring it runs terminal in backend"*
- Approval: AP-172 APPLIED (ADR-265)
- Dependencies: none

- NOT LANDED, and the reason: **no browser evidence**. The standalone API requires verified auth, and the desktop shell that supplies it renders in a Tauri window this session could not screenshot. Every assertion above is proven at the router, none in a running UI. Tier C wants the browser leg seen before this closes.
- WIRED 2026-09-02 (same-run wiring pass): `changedPaths` is no longer dropped. A turn whose backend edited files appends one `chat_backend_changed_files` ledger row (paths only, never contents) and carries it as a `result` ref on the assistant turn, asserted in `chat-agentic-backend.test.ts`.
- Also open: the reply is still truncated to the 8k the turn column allows.

## The Builder Agent executes: primitive executor, loop, and the execution-first gate (BA0, first half)

- ID: TASK-091
- Status: in_progress (landed 2026-09-02; BA0's containment and ledger criteria are NOT met — see TASK-092)
- Priority: P1
- Horizon: Living Software
- Outcome: the Capability Builder stops being a text-drafting persona and gains the machinery to act — a gate that returns execute/approve/refuse, a executor that actually reads, writes, edits and runs commands inside one Module directory, and a loop that drives them through Bridge's own `ModelProvider`. Ordinary work runs without asking; only genuinely risky calls stop for a human; a short absolute list can never run at all.
- Prototype test: give the Builder a task in a scratch Module, watch it write a file and run a command with no approval prompt, watch a `curl` escalate to a Proposal, and watch `git push` refuse with the branch-and-merge reason. Confirm every one of the three appears in the Run's audit trail.
- Scope: `packages/core/src/capability/primitive-policy.ts`, `packages/core/src/capability/builder-loop.ts`, `apps/api/src/builder/primitive-executor.ts`, and their tests.
- Evidence: `packages/core/src/capability/builder-primitives.ts` has carried the risk lookup, grant-scope check and SandboxProvider port since Track F2 with **no caller anywhere in `apps/api`** — classification without execution. myzazoo's `src/hook.ts` + `src/tools.ts` + `src/loop.ts` are the working reference for the executing half.
- Requests: user directives 2026-09-02, verbatim: *"ensure we have the builder agent completely built"* and *"I want governance but not at cost of execution. Seek approval only if high risk task, else lets have exectuion first approach.Bridge needs an agentic backend similar to my zazoo and I hope capabilities are designed as swappable elements"*
- Approval: AP-172 APPLIED (ADR-266)
- Dependencies: none
- LANDED 2026-09-02: 31 tests green across the three modules (13 policy, 10 executor, 8 loop), including the seeded escapes that matter most — a denied command chained behind an allowed one, a path traversal out of the working directory, and an attempt to read the API process's env.
- WIRED 2026-09-02: `builder.run` now calls them. See TASK-092.

## BA0 completion: governed Builder Runs, ledger receipts, and the container sandbox for untrusted bodies

- ID: TASK-092
- Status: in_progress (the Run seam and its receipts landed 2026-09-02; the container adapter has NOT)
- Priority: P1
- Horizon: Living Software
- Outcome: BA0's own exit criteria are met — every Builder action lands in the immutable ledger with model/prompt versions and token cost attached; a seeded containment suite runs as a permanent CI gate; and a real container/microVM `SandboxProvider` exists for UNTRUSTED capability bodies (Commons imports, foreign imports, Builder-generated executable code), which the host executor deliberately does not cover.
- Prototype test: run a Builder task end to end from a user request and read its ledger — one row per action with cost. Then feed the containment suite a Commons capability that tries to reach the network and confirm the sandbox blocks it and the block is recorded. `NotImplementedContainerSandboxProvider` must no longer be the only container adapter.
- Scope: `apps/api` wiring for a Builder Run, `packages/core/src/capability/sandbox-provider.ts`'s missing adapter, CI.
- Evidence: `docs/raw/builder-agent-execution-plan-2026-09.md` §6 lists this as the largest unbuilt piece and BA2's hard dependency.
- Approval: AP-172 APPLIED (ADR-266)
- Dependencies: TASK-091
- LANDED 2026-09-02: `apps/api/src/builder/run.ts` + the `builder.run` procedure — Module governance decides whether the Builder may run at all (`builder.run`, refusing with the user's own stated reason), every primitive call appends a ledger row whichever way it went, and the Run closes with one receipt row carrying stop reason, model, model calls and token counts. `runBuilderLoop` now accumulates per-step and per-run usage so that receipt is real rather than an empty field. `builder.` is Local-Plane-only in `deployment-boundary.ts`. `apps/api/test/builder-run.test.ts` (3 tests, green): a file really written to disk with a costed receipt, `git push` refused mid-run and still recorded, and a Module policy denying `builder.run` refusing in the user's own words.
- NOT LANDED: the container/microVM `SandboxProvider` for UNTRUSTED bodies, and the permanent CI containment suite. Both remain BA2's hard dependency. Also no approval round-trip: a call needing approval stops the Run and is reported (BA4 owns the resume).
- PromptAssembler v1 is specified by BOTH Builder BA0 and Learning LA1 and must be ONE implementation. Whichever slice lands first builds it; the other consumes it. Unassigned as of 2026-09-02 — do not build a second one.

## The conversation survives a model switch, and each Module reopens its own session

- ID: TASK-093
- Status: in_progress (built and wired 2026-09-03; only the browser leg of the exit test is outstanding — TASK-041)
- Priority: P1
- Horizon: Living Software
- Outcome: switching the model in the Chat panel keeps the conversation — same thread, same turns, and the incoming engine is handed what was already said. Opening a Module reopens that Module's own live conversation with its history, and a session can attach further Modules so one conversation can span several.
- Prototype test: send a message on the Local model, switch to Claude Code, and see the earlier turns still on screen; ask "what did I just say?" and get an answer that proves the context carried. Open a Module, talk, leave, reopen it, and land back in the same conversation. Attach a second Module and confirm both are listed on the thread.
- Scope: `chat.thread.setBackend` / `forModule` / `attachModule`, `priorTurnsTranscript`, `chat_threads.module_name` + `attached_modules` (migration 0045), `useChat`'s `switchBackend`/`openModuleChat`/`attachModule`, the ChatView model menu.
- Evidence: user-reported, 2026-09-02, verbatim: *"When i change AI models in left hand chat, the chat should remain consistent since Bridge is managing context and should direct the chat to a given model and all models remain active in backend for quick swap. Currently the chat clears when I switch models. Also Each modules continues from previous session that is associated with given module just like claude code sessions are associated with projects but I can add multiple projects to a given session"*
- Approval: AP-173 APPLIED (ADR-267)
- Dependencies: TASK-090
- The clearing predates the agentic backend: the plane selector has called `newChat` since it shipped, so Local↔Cloud discarded the conversation too. Fixed for both.
- LANDED 2026-09-02: the three procedures, the transcript carry, the migration, and `apps/api/test/chat-agentic-backend.test.ts` (5 tests) — including the decisive one, which asserts the carried prompt contains the earlier turn verbatim.
- WIRED 2026-09-02: `Layout` computes the active Module from the nav route (longest matching base) and passes it through `AgentPanel` → `ChatView` → `useChat(surface, moduleName)`, whose init effect calls `openModuleChat`. The Right Chat Panel on a Module now opens that Module's own live thread.
- WIRED 2026-09-03: `attachModule` now has a control. The composer carries an "Attach a Module" select beside the model select, offering only Modules this Organization has installed and available (the same `modules.list` filter the nav uses, so the offer matches what a user can actually open) and hiding the ones already on the thread; the Modules on the conversation render as badges above the composer, which is the half of the exit test that says "confirm both are listed on the thread". Verified: `chat.test.mjs` 8/8 including a new TASK-093 case, web suite green, web typecheck clean.
- NOT LANDED: no browser evidence, same reason as TASK-090 — the on-screen half of the exit test needs the user's machine (TASK-041). Noted rather than guarded: `forModule`/`attachModule` accept any Module name string server-side. That is harmless while the name is a durable LABEL on the thread with no filesystem or authority effect (grep: nothing but `liveModuleThread` reads it), and the UI only offers installed Modules — but the moment a Module name selects that Module's files or authority for an agentic backend, both procedures need an installed-Module check, in one shared guard rather than two.
- 2026-09-05: the "On this conversation:" badge line was read by the user as Agents (BUGS 2026-09-05 — *"Why does it show on this conversation task manager? If its showing agents involved…"*); it now reads "Working in:" with the Module explanation on the element, and `@` in the composer is where Agents live — TASK-101.
## The Builder Agent acts as itself — an attributable Run behind both Builder lanes

- ID: TASK-094
- Status: done (2026-09-02, AP-181/ADR-276)
- Priority: P2
- Estimate: 2d
- Horizon: Hardening
- Outcome: When the Capability Builder drafts steps (rung 3) or proposes a structure (rung 4), the work is attributed to `CAPABILITY_BUILDER_AGENT` as a real Agent Run under its own role and scope, rather than executing as whichever human pressed the button. The Run is inspectable afterwards and says what the Builder read and what it proposed.
- Prototype test: press "Draft steps from my decisions" and "Show the shape of what Bridge has observed"; each leaves an Agent Run attributed to the Capability Builder, visible in the Runs surface, carrying the evidence ids the proposal was derived from. Narrowing the Builder's scope below what the lane reads makes the Run fail closed rather than silently succeeding as the human.
- Scope: `apps/api/src/router.ts` (`learning.promotions.drafts.proposeSteps`, `learning.claims.proposeStructure`), `wiring.ts`'s `CAPABILITY_BUILDER_AGENT` role/scope, the Automation Run recorder. Do NOT change the derivations themselves — `builder.ts` stays pure.
- Evidence: found 2026-09-02 while building rung 4 (ADR-275). `wiring.ts` seeds the agent identity, `role-capability-builder`, a `signal:write` scope and `ensureCapabilityBuilderGovernance` in both the hosted and local wirings; neither Builder lane references any of it. This is a real gap against CLAUDE.md's "only an attributable allowed Agent invokes them", and it predates rung 4 — rung 3 has had it since 2026-08-13.
- Requests: user directive 2026-09-02, verbatim: *"complete the wiring and associated pending tasks"* — the wiring completed there was rung 4's; this is what that work uncovered and deliberately did not fold in.
- Approval: AP-180 records the finding; the fix itself is unapproved work.
- Dependencies: TASK-053 (done)
- LANDED 2026-09-02: `runAsCapabilityBuilder` wraps both lanes — `resolveAuthority` for the Builder as actor on behalf of the requesting human, then an Agent Run under `CAPABILITY_BUILDER_AGENT` finished `completed` with the evidence ids derived (or `halted` with the error). `learning.builderRuns` reads them back; both lanes return their `runId` and both surfaces show it. `ensureTaskManagerAutomation` gained an optional Goal type instead of a near-duplicate, because `automation_runs` has a composite FK to `automations` and the Builder needed a real Automation row. Exit test met: api `builder-attribution` 4/4 — the Run exists, is attributed to the Builder and not the human, carries live claim ids, is listable, records a refusal as `completed` with its reason, and **setting the Builder's scope to `[]` fails the lane closed** while the human's own authority is untouched. That negative case is mutation-checked: deleting the authority guard turns it RED and leaves the other three green. Existing lanes unaffected: `builder-steps` 4/4, `builder-structure` 4/4, `procedure-classification` 5/5.
- NOT DONE, and named rather than left implicit: the Builder's Runs carry no taint label (the recorder supports one), and no web surface lists them — `learning.builderRuns` exists and is tested, but nothing in the app calls it. The data is queryable; the screen is not built.

## The Avatar has hands — Hey Clicky parity: a real pointer that glides, clicks, and types, behind explicit control consent
- ID: TASK-095
- Status: in_progress (2026-09-03, AP-182/ADR-277)
- Priority: P1
- Estimate: 4d
- Horizon: Prototype
- Outcome: The desktop Avatar does what Hey Clicky does: given a task, it looks at the consented screen, decides one action at a time, glides the REAL macOS pointer along a human-looking arc, clicks, types, presses allowlisted keys, scrolls, and opens apps, narrating each step, until the task is done, refused, stopped, or bounded — and its on-screen pointer is a proper arrow glyph that flies (the clicky flight arc) instead of a ring. Control is opt-in per request, Accessibility-gated, privacy-guarded every step, and the user's hand always wins: moving the mouse mid-glide halts the run.
- Prototype test: On a real desktop build with Screen Recording + Accessibility granted and a Groq key, open the companion panel → Do, turn on "Let Zazoo use my mouse and keyboard", type "open Notes and write hello" → the arrow glyph and the real cursor glide to the Notes icon/field, a click ring flashes, text is typed, the step list narrates each step with ✓, and the run ends "Done". Move the mouse during a glide → the run stops within a tick and says so. Turn the switch off → `act_start` refuses with `ACT_CONTROL_NOT_ALLOWED` and nothing moves. Revoke Accessibility → refuses with `ACT_NO_ACCESSIBILITY` and prompts. A password-manager window frontmost → the run halts before capture.
- Scope: `actuator.rs` (CGEvent move/click/type/key/scroll, smoothstep Bézier glide, takeover detection), `act.rs` (bounded look-decide-act loop, closed planner vocabulary, `act_start/act_poll/act_stop`), `AgentPointer.tsx` (arrow glyph + flight), `DoRun.tsx` + Do mode in `CompanionAsk.tsx`, `companion_capabilities.accessibility`, `monitor_logical_rect`. Reuse: TASK-027's capture, two-stage grid locator, typed marks, Groq vision, `say` TTS; TASK-054's Accessibility permission check/prompt.
- Evidence: research 2026-09-03 (Hey Clicky changelog to v1.0.48; farzaa/clicky, jasonkneen/openclicky, unn-Known1/clickyX, trycua/cua-driver, UI-TARS, self-operating-computer): Clicky's visible cursor is a drawn overlay flying a smoothstep Bézier; real input goes through a per-window background driver that never moves the real cursor. No clone with a visible cursor moves the real pointer smoothly — Bridge does, on purpose, because a visible real cursor is the honest tell. Behaviors re-derived, no source copied (clicky is MIT Swift; Bridge is Rust/Tauri). Verification: Rust 195 passed + 1 ignored (11 new; clippy clean on the new files); two guards mutation-checked RED-then-green (⌘Q refusal, click-without-cell refusal); web typecheck 0 errors, 117/117 tests, production build, ui-rules and vocabulary gates pass; browser lab (`annotate.html?lab=1`) proves the arrow renders, the click ring centres on its tip, and a throttled flight still lands on target. No live desktop run — no cloud call and no real input event was posted this session; the at-keyboard walk is the user's step.
- Requests: user directive 2026-09-03, verbatim: *"I want avatar to be fully functional and be able to do every task hey clicky does. Refer opensource hey clicky alternatives and similar clone repos. I hope implementations of mouse pointer from clicky avatar can be ported. I dont need circular pointers but proper mouse pointer"*
- Approval: AP-182 applied (in-session directive) for building the actuator ahead of TASK-056's separate per-app allowlist design; the allowlist decision stays open there
- Dependencies: TASK-027 (done); TASK-054 (Accessibility permission surface, done part)
- GAPS CLOSED (2026-09-03, same day): **guided walkthroughs** — "Show me how" runs the same planner in guide mode (nothing posted, no control consent needed): each step is pointed at with the arrow + spotlight, narrated/spoken, and the loop waits for the USER's own click near the target (permission-free `CGEventSourceButtonState` poll, one "not quite" nudge, 90 s per step); **circle-to-focus** — "Circle an area" makes the click-through annotate window interactive for one drag, the rectangle is drawn into the consented screenshot as a red outline and named in the prompt, so pointing coordinates stay full-frame; **dictation into the focused app** — "Dictate into my app": what you say with Fn held is transcribed and typed via `act_type_text` (same consent/Accessibility/privacy-guard gates, 500-char cap); **per-app allowlist** — "Only act in these apps" (substring, case-insensitive, empty = any per ADR-263), checked before every step. Verification: Rust 196 passed (+1: allowlist), web typecheck 0 errors, 238/238 tests, ui-rules and vocabulary gates pass.
- NOT LANDED (stated): a background per-window driver that acts without your cursor (refused by design — invisible actuation); Skills from the panel (TASK-057) — the overlay's existing Chat panel already reaches governed Skills, so no duplicate surface was built; always-on wake word (refused — no silent sensing). Live at-keyboard walk of the prototype test is the user's step.

## The companion appears only when summoned: shortcut or open panel, never hover or launch
- ID: TASK-099
- Status: done (2026-09-04; PR #80 merged, installer rebuilt; user confirmation on the rebuilt app still owed for the Fn hold and auto-dismiss)
- Priority: P0
- Horizon: Living Software
- Outcome: The desktop companion is concealed at rest in both homes and appears only on ⌘⇧Space or while a panel it opened is up; moving the cursor over the notch, finishing launch, or an arrow/Home/End/F-key while typing never presents it; a held Fn (≈0.4 s) still does, and a push-to-talk request dismisses itself once the dictation is typed or the answer is delivered and spoken.

- Evidence: typecheck + `cargo check`; the two presentation effects share the `summoned` gate and `bridge:companion-ptt` is emitted only by the ⌘⇧Space global shortcut (`lib.rs`) and a sustained Fn hold (`notch.rs`). No automated test drives the macOS flag poll; verified by reasoning on NSEvent flag semantics, to be confirmed on the rebuilt installer. A UI-rules doc gate for the companion is not in place.
- Requests: user report 2026-09-04 verbatim in BUGS 2026-09-04 "the companion avatar activated on its own".

## `@` in Chat addresses another Agent; the footer names Modules as Modules
- ID: TASK-101
- Status: in_progress (built and wired 2026-09-05; the per-Agent lane handoff and the browser leg are NOT LANDED — see below)
- Priority: P1
- Horizon: Living Software
- Outcome: Chief of Staff is the only Agent the Chat panel puts in front of a person. The line under the composer says which Modules the conversation is working in and that they are Modules; typing `@` in the composer lists the Organization's active Agents from a real read, inserting one addresses that Agent, the turn records who was addressed, and the reply says who answered. When an Agent has its own lane (Learning Agent → research start, Capability Builder → Builder Run) the addressed turn is handed to it; until then Chief of Staff answers on its behalf and the turn says so.
- Prototype test: on a Module's Chat, read "Working in: TaskManager" with the Module explanation on hover; type `@le`, see "Learning Agent" with its one-line role, press Enter, see `@Learning Agent ` in the draft; send and see the reply badged "Addressed to Learning Agent · answered by Chief of Staff"; call `turn.send` with a `mentions` id that is not an active Agent of the Organization and see it refused with no turn written.
- Scope: `apps/web/src/app/chat/ChatView.tsx` (footer copy, `@` picker, addressed badge), `apps/web/src/app/chat/useChat.ts` (`send(message, mentions)`), `apps/api/src/routers/chat.ts` (`chat.agents.list`; mention check and `addressed_agent` refs in `turn.send`), `apps/api/src/router-shared.ts` (`chatSendInput.mentions`), `packages/core/src/chat-store.ts` + `packages/db` migration 0050 (`addressed_agent` ref kind).
- Evidence: `apps/web/test/chat.test.mjs` (the footer no longer says "On this conversation"; the `@` picker is bound to `chat.agents.list`, has the listbox and the Escape/ArrowDown cases, and the send carries `mentions` — seen failing 2/9 before the change, 9/9 after); `apps/api/test/chat-mentions.test.ts` (2 tests: the picker lists the Learning Agent with a role and never Chief of Staff, every row an active Agent of the Organization; `turn.send` with `mentions` records the `addressed_agent` ref on the assistant turn, and a stranger id is refused with no turn written). api + web typecheck clean; vocabulary and UI-rules gates.
- Requests: user directive 2026-09-05, verbatim: *"Why does it show on this conversation task manager? If its showing agents involved, should the user be able to pick and choose agents in which case its multi select dropdown. Also is task manager handling the work of the builder? Because I thought chief of staff will be user facing and then it internally directs to different agents based on need. WHy do we even need to show other agent? If user wants to call other agents, pressing @ in chatbot should show agents that can be called"*
- Approval: none needed (additive ref kind on an existing table; ADR 2026-09-05 "`@` addresses an Agent; Chief of Staff stays the face" records it).
- Dependencies: TASK-093
- NOT LANDED: (1) **the lane handoff.** A mention is recorded and shown, but Chief of Staff still answers every turn — `chat.turn.send` starts no research Run for `@Learning Agent` and no Builder Run for `@Capability Builder`. Those lanes exist (`agentOrchestration.research.*`, `builder.run`) with their own lifecycles — a Run with receipts, a Module directory — and handing a chat turn into either is a new seam, not a routing that exists today; `chiefOfStaff.converse` still carries the older text-parsed `@learning`/`@builder` route (ADR-033/046, `invokeAgent`) that the Chat panel never calls. When the handoff lands it is one seam for both, not two. (2) The model sees only the literal `@Name` in the message, so its prose may not say it answered on that Agent's behalf — the badge on the turn does. (3) No browser evidence, same reason as TASK-090/093 (TASK-041). (4) A retry re-sends the content without `mentions`; the original `addressed_agent` ref on the assistant turn is kept.
